# i2pr Active Planning Registry

Compact control surface for active planning. Detailed requirements and completed history
remain in subsystem roadmaps, implementation plans, `plans/closure/`, and Git history.
Projected from the frozen `plans/archive/legacy-flat-registry-2026-09-18.md` on 2026-09-18;
the i2pr token in each closure record stays authoritative, `state` is the codegg projection.

Canonical direction remains in `GUARDRAILS.md`, `specs/CONFORMANCE.md`,
`specs/support.toml`, and `docs/adr/`.

## Status vocabulary projection

| i2pr token shape | Codegg state | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `passed-*` | `closed` | Closure record accepted; evidence landed |
| `retained-*` (no `superseded-by`) | `retained` | Evidence kept; interpretation narrowed |
| `*-superseded-by-planNNN` | `superseded` | Replaced; see the superseding plan |
| `blocked-*`, `stopped-*` | `blocked` | Named dependency/evidence gap prevents progress |
| `in-progress-*` | `active` | Implementation or closure work in progress |
| `registered-*`, `pending`, `next *` | `ready` | Approved for execution when dependencies clear |
| `skipped`, `retired*` | `archived` | Never authoritative; retained for traceability |

## Core-router operational recovery — registered 2026-10-10

The new [core-router recovery roadmap](subsystems/core-router-recovery-roadmap.md) explicitly includes **NTCP2 and optional floodfill** as completion requirements, not indefinitely deferred enhancements. Plan 430 passed as an infrastructure and invariant baseline; Plan 432 then delivered consented, verified HTTPS SU3 cold-start reseeding. Neither changed normal public network posture or support claims. Normal public SSU2/NTCP2, transit, and `caps=f` remain unqualified. Historical Plan 099/101, 161, 268, 303/306, and Proposal-170 ELS2 closure tokens are unchanged. Plan 431 is stopped and Plan 432 passed. Corrective Plans 410, 414–424 stopped at their recorded evidence boundaries. Plan 420 reached the post-validation RouterInfo marker but saw no I2NP block or DeliveryStatus; the termination count could not be tied to that accepted session without retaining identity-bearing data. Plan 424 later localized the reverse responder failure to a closed SessionConfirmed read but could not correlate it to the helper's session-not-established result. The bounded corrective chain is stopped. Plans 433–439 remain gated on their registered evidence.

### Emissary-informed qualification corrective sequence — Plans 440–444 (registered; no capabilities promoted)

Research: [read-only pinned Emissary comparison](diagnostics/2026-10-10-emissary-router-qualification-comparison.md). Actual Emissary transport tests allow loopback with distinct router identities and scoped `allow_local`; loopback **does not inherently invalidate** transport evidence, but can invalidate stock-peer selection, reachable-R/public-address and tunnel-bootstrap assumptions. The old NTCP2 micro-diagnostic chain remains stopped; no earlier status token is rewritten. Controlled integration and early capacity-class engineering are intentionally independent of external LAN and transit completion, whereas full normal/public roles are not.

With Plan 440 closed, Plan 442 completed its local durable-key owner and loopback lifecycle evidence, then stopped at the unavailable authorized independent LAN pair; Plan 431 remains stopped. Plan 443's controlled one-family daemon-owned component lanes pass, but its normal-process and stock-to-stock control gate remains open; Plan 433 stays blocked. Plan 454 closed the shared-rate owner and capacity permit. Plan 444 then added the Java N-class floodfill floor but remains blocked on a source-pinned Java candidate-preference control and real store/lookup evidence; its historical blocked closure remains unchanged. Plan 441 is blocked after Plan 445 corrected its missing failure attribution but the stock-to-stock positive control still failed (`control-*-session-not-established`). Existing Plans 433–439 keep their recorded public/full-feature acceptance prerequisites. No direct public networking, no modified reference routers, no fabricated caps, and no historical closure rewrites.

### NTCP2-first corrective handoff — Plans 455–458 (registered 2026-10-10)

Owner priority: **NTCP2 protocol interoperability and real-daemon activation outrank SAM product integration, Java floodfill selection, and further general qualification**. Plan 452 has an admissible, correctly isolated two-i2pd reference configuration but observed **no outbound dial trigger**; i2pd's pinned `Transports::SendMessage -> PostMessages -> ConnectToPeer` source confirms a legitimate I2NP send is required. Therefore no new attempt should be classified as a Noise/NTCP2 failure before a selected send exists. Plan 101 default-disabled normal NTCP2 and `specs/support.toml` remain unchanged on registration.

| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **455** | **blocked** | [Authenticated initiated NTCP2 + real stock outbound workload](implementation/ntcp2-transport/455-initiated-i2np-and-stock-peer-workload-recovery.md) | `closure/ntcp2-transport/455-status.md` (`blocked-stock-to-i2pr-and-negative-matrix-unproven`): Gates A/B passed; stock-to-i2pr delivery and the negative matrix remain open. |
| **456** | **closed — controlled source/lifecycle pass** | [Guarded normal-daemon NTCP2 transport owner](implementation/ntcp2-transport/456-guarded-normal-daemon-ntcp2-service-owner.md) | `closure/ntcp2-transport/456-status.md`: real controlled-loopback owner, signed RouterInfo/key lifecycle, wrong-network rejection, matching-peer authentication, shared governor/dispatch; no external qualification claim. |
| **457** | **blocked on 455 — P1** | [Normal-process NTCP2 I2NP + SSU2 fallback](implementation/ntcp2-transport/457-real-process-ntcp2-i2np-and-ssu2-fallback-acceptance.md) | `closure/ntcp2-transport/457-status.md` (`blocked-plan455-stock-to-i2pr-and-dual-family-continuity`): Plan 456 and Plan 455 Gates A/B passed; stock-to-i2pr delivery and dual-family continuity remain open. |
| **458** | **blocked — P2** | [Normal SAM remote-product composition](implementation/core-router-recovery/458-remote-sam-product-composition-corrective.md) | `closure/core-router-recovery/458-status.md` (`blocked-real-process-nonzero-hop-sam-exchange-unproven`): rejected submissions and exact-attempt invalid replies (including wrapped type-19 replies, covered by a deterministic correlation regression) are correlated; latest ordinary `i2pr run` still times out at outbound build before any stream. |

**Bounded anti-loop policy:** Plan 455 explicitly separates direct i2pr→i2pd proof from diagnosing stock peer-selection, uses real router-generated messages rather than an idle listener, and permits a fixture rerun after a *specific confirmed correction*. Do not issue another observer-only plan for the same untriggered control; do not suppress the i2pr-initiated lane just because an unrelated stock-to-stock control cannot select a peer. Verify stage and I2NP receipts from independent endpoints; no helper, patched stock router, public I2P activity, or fabricated class/reachability. Close into new, immutable `plans/closure/ntcp2-transport/455-status.md`, `456-status.md`, `457-status.md` and `plans/closure/core-router-recovery/458-status.md` only on executed evidence. Plans 434/435/451/452 remain blocked until their own stated missing evidence actually closes. Plan 444 Java fixture is already registered and remains lower priority; avoid duplicating it as a new plan.

### NTCP2 source-grounded continuation and execution-loop repair — Plans 467–469 (registered 2026-10-11)

**Authority:** Plan 459's bounded shared ingress and normal responder-side RouterInfo effect landed; normal daemon-initiated product receipt and a correlated reply remain **blocked**. Plan 455 passed independent i2pr→stock decode and stock-to-stock NTCP2 application controls. Plan 463's actual `caps=f` controlled role landed but its single seeded RouterInfo was not selected by pinned i2pd `Explore`; no stock reverse handshake occurred. Historical records stay immutable.

The new [planning execution policy](README.md#bounded-execution-and-causal-corrective-policy) clarifies that a source-identified fixture or product bug must be fixed, regression-tested and re-run **inside the active workstream**; only new owner/scope or an already finalized blocked milestone needs a new plan. This prevents repeated numbered observer and logging-only follow-ups while preserving hard conformance and security gates.

### Functional-router continuation — Plans 470–473 (registered 2026-10-12)

### Burst-default policy corrective — Plan 484 (registered 2026-10-15)

Carried forward from the 479 finding and the 483 limitation: the 64 KiB
daemon default `burst_bytes` is below the NTCP2 runtime's wire-frame
minimum (`72819` at 10% reserve), so a bandwidth-enabled daemon fails
construction at defaults. One coherent policy pass, decision-provable
offline: raise the default to fit, or keep it and fail closed naming both
values. No silent default change, no lowered wire minimum, no live lane.

### SAM inbound-SYN routing corrective — Plan 486 (registered 2026-10-16)

Carried forward from Plan 478's lane `m8v2xk5q` 10-key ACCEPT-SYN receipt: inbound SYNs authenticate and dispatch to the bridge canonical manager (`GenericClient` profile), which holds no listener, while `STREAM ACCEPT` binds wildcard-0 and polls backlog on the receiver mirror — so the waiter can never succeed and stock retries 10× then terminates. One coherent routing pass: waiter-scoped SYN routing to the mirror, stale-backlog age eviction, per-instance receipt split, silent-drop invariant. Considered and rejected: always-feed-mirror type-split (forward-leg blast radius), canonical bind (pump re-thread audit surface), server-kind flip (server-machinery enrollment, new loopback surface), timeout lengthening, transit hunting. No support/capability/advertisement change; no stock patching.

WP2 the four-stage per-stage attribution surface (manager arrival, ACK generated, ACK transmitted, ACK egress) is landed, each stage counted by its own counter, with `ack_no_ack_emitted` as a permanent negative control, and a **live** one-shot receipt that is now emitted by the mode that owns the destination (it was dead in router-network mode); WP5 the ELS2 Plan 393 three-failure-class cancellation property is **made true** (the publication intent is reserved *before* the record is composed, and the compose, request-construction, and delivery classes each release it at their own site) — `check-els2-live-lane-evidence.sh` is green with no duplicated call site and no script change; Every owner lookup therefore missed and every established-phase packet fell back to profile routing, so the inbound DATA of an accepted connection was routed to the canonical manager where no application reads it — the packet was rejected, never acknowledged, and the stock peer exhausted its retransmissions. The peek now reports `peek.send_stream_id`; the first regression test was **degenerate** (it built the packet with the two ids swapped, and both ids start at `0x8000_0000`, so the swap was invisible) and now burns one originator id first and asserts the ids differ. Also fixed: the approaching-expiry rotation re-signed an **unchanged** lease set, which ended in `LeaseAlreadyExpired` and failed the `ssu2-router` service — the margin is now the replenishment deadline, a rotation never re-signs an unchanged set, and a lease-refresh failure is destination-scoped. **Live:** the pinned lane passes in **both** directions (`i2pr-to-i2pd` 1816 bytes, `i2pd-to-i2pr` 2328 bytes, both digest-matched) with **zero** `packet was not ACKed after 10 attempts` in the stock peer, and the §6 gate is qualified in the same run: lease rotation `rotated: true` (`published` +540 s, oldest-lease expiry +420 000 ms, the rotated record acknowledged by a correlated `DeliveryStatus` **on an inbound tunnel**, the same payload digest before and after the rotation, `service_failures: {}`), and the daemon restart with the persisted identity and the router **hash** unchanged and a fresh destination reachable on the first attempt. **Recorded, not fixed:** F2 the session's control-socket bound, **F3 is discharged conditionally by Plan 512**: the destination inbound pool now has the reference's pair-level failure detection, so a destination inbound tunnel whose path stops answering is failed, replaced and re-advertised without waiting for the lease to expire; the source-side work is complete and proven in-tree, and what remains is the **stock-peer** half -- a two-host run against a pinned stock router with a real dead transit peer -- which is blocked externally on Plan 493's authorized topology, F6 the accept socket's single bidirectional channel (the pinned reference shares it). 478 is closed with this plan. No support/capability/advertisement change. |
| **510** | **closed** | [The managed-tunnel staging path under load, and its diagnosability](implementation/core-router-recovery/510-managed-tunnel-staging-under-load.md) | Predecessor: Plan 491 (**F4** -- the intermittent `i2pcontrol_tunnels` managed-tunnel staging failure), Plan 487, Plan 289 (the TunnelManager black-box lane). **The gap:** Plan 491's floor reported `tunnelmanager_emits_canonical_proposal_result_and_redacts_secrets` failing with *runtime transition failed: manager configuration rejected*. It did not reproduce in three isolated runs, in the full `--no-fail-fast` workspace sweep, or in the two later floor runs, and Plan 491 recorded the residual risk as *a load-sensitive race in the managed-service staging path*, left for a plan-of-record because the path belongs to the I2PControl / service-tunnel line. The failure was also **undiagnosable**: the daemon's `static_manager_reason` maps every `ServiceTunnelError::InvalidConfig(_)` to the single label *manager configuration rejected* and discards the detail, where the class actually lives. **The plan delivers both halves.** A **value-free reason classification** maps the detail to a stable class by a pinned marker list, so a rejection names its class and the operator's view never echoes a value; an unknown detail keeps today's generic label, so the change can never lose information. A **load-sensitive black-box lane** drives N concurrent managed-tunnel creates against one real daemon -- each create generating a destination identity and building a lease set, so the load is real work and not a sleep -- and asserts that every create succeeds, with a contradictory configuration rejected in the same lane so the reject path is exercised rather than assumed. The lane's load is real work, its concurrency is bounded, and every wait is an explicit deadline rather than a wall-clock sleep. The reason string is produced by the daemon and is **not** pinned by any fixture, spec, contract crate or other test. **Advertises nothing** -- `router.version` stays `0.9.62`, `caps` and the published RouterInfo are unchanged, the new support entry is `experimental` with `advertised = false`, and `Cargo.toml` / `Cargo.lock` stay byte-identical. **Not closed here:** Plan 491 F2 and F5, Plan 487 F3, Plan 497 limitation 5's read half, and every externally-blocked plan  **Closed as `passed-managed-tunnel-staging-under-load`.** Plan 491's **F4** is **discharged conditionally**: the failure did **not** reproduce under the new lane, and that is recorded plainly rather than claimed as a fix. What the plan delivers unconditionally is the two things Plan 491 asked for. **The classification:** `static_manager_reason`'s `InvalidConfig` arm maps the daemon's own detail to a **value-free class** through a pinned list of forty (marker, class) pairs. The marker is a stable phrase *inside* the detail -- the service id is embedded at the front of most messages, so a prefix match would have to know the id, and a class that depends on the id is not a class -- and the class is a short label of letters and spaces. A detail that matches no marker keeps today's single generic label, so the change can never make a rejection *less* informative than it was. Both the classifier and the reason mapper return `&'static str`, so the **type system** forbids a detail from reaching the wire; the reason template is fixed (`runtime transition failed: {reason}`), a class plus a prefix. **The load lane:** `crates/i2pr-daemon/tests/i2pcontrol_tunnels_load.rs` drives **eight concurrent** managed-tunnel creates against one real daemon, each with its own name, loopback port and target destination, and each generating a destination identity, building a lease set and binding a listener. The load is **real work**, never a sleep; every wait is an explicit bounded deadline; the concurrency is bounded. The same lane exercises the **reject** path -- a server kind with `UseSSL` and no daemon TLS policy, refused while the shared manager stages the runtime -- and asserts that the reason names a class and carries no value, and that the shared manager still stages a runtime afterwards. **The guard:** `crates/i2pr-daemon/tests/managed_tunnel_reason_boundary.rs` pins the table (non-empty, no duplicate marker or class, no shadowed marker), the classification's totality, the agreement between the pinned table and the production function, the value-freeness of every class, the **liveness** of every marker in the daemon's own error construction, the `&'static str` signatures, the fixed reason template, and the lane's boundedness, concurrency and freedom from sleeps; every rule carries an inverted probe. **The controls are not vacuous, and that is measured:** seven mutations of the real tree, each applied, run against the real lanes, and reverted byte-for-byte -- a collapsed `InvalidConfig` arm fails five tests, a value-bearing class fails seven, an unreachable generic fallback fails four, a reworded construction message fails four, a neutered concurrency bound fails four, a sleeping lane fails three, and a drifted marker fails five; none broke the build. Two **weaknesses** were found and fixed rather than absorbed: the control detector's build-failure matcher was **too loose** on its first run -- it matched the test runner's own `error: test failed` footer, so every control's count was zero and the run was inconclusive -- and it was tightened to accept only a compiler error or an abort before the controls were re-run; and the lane's fixed port band overlapped the operating system's ephemeral range, which the controls surfaced as a 5.4-second wobble in a lane that otherwise runs in 0.57 seconds, and the band was moved below the ephemeral range. **Advertises nothing** -- `router.version` stays `0.9.62`, `caps` and the published RouterInfo are unchanged, the new support entry **is** `experimental` with `advertised = false`, the inventory's advertised count stays **zero** (102 surfaces, 96 experimental), and `Cargo.toml` / `Cargo.lock` stay byte-identical. **The condition on the close:** F4 is closed on the conditional basis that the path now has a load-sensitive lane and a future occurrence names its class; the exact future evidence that would close it unconditionally is a reproduction together with the class the new reason reports. **Plan 491's closure record is not rewritten** -- the discharge lives here, in Plan 510's closure record, and in the roadmap row.|
| **511** | **closed** | [The comparator's component ceiling, derived rather than assumed](implementation/core-router-recovery/511-version-component-ceiling.md) | Plan 491's **F5** -- *"The comparator's four-component ceiling is an unstated assumption in the specification's terms"* -- was recorded and not fixed, on two grounds the plan finds wrong: that *"widening the ceiling is a one-line change"* (it is compile-breaking at three sites, and it multiplies the stored array by 26), and that *"the plan's scope was the current table"* (the current table is the widest row, which is exactly the assumption F5 names). The tree holds the specification's **minimum-peer obligation** rows as data (`MINIMUM_PEER_OBLIGATIONS`, six rows, all three components) but does **not** hold the specification's feature-support rows, so the four-component row the ceiling rests on (`0.6.1.10`) appears in the tree only as **prose** in a doc comment. Plan 511 makes that row a **held record** and the ceiling a **derivation** from it, so a future five-component row in the specification's table widens the comparator by construction -- the version becomes **comparable**, which is exactly what F5 says the current design denies -- instead of becoming uncomparable behind a hard-coded four. **No production behaviour change**: the record is four components wide, so the comparator accepts and refuses exactly the versions it accepts and refuses today; what changes is that the number now has a **source**, and the ordering stays **exact** because the stored array and the parser's bound remain the same constant. The plan also records, for the first time in this repository, what the two **pinned references** actually do with a version that has more components than the specification's table has ever used: **i2pd 2.61.0** (pin `635b013a612ff47278ef02acf8580a28e10e26c5`, `libi2pd/RouterInfo.cpp`) collapses every digit of `router.version` into an `int` with **no component count**, so `"0.9.62.1.2"` becomes `96212` and is **above** the floor `962` (`libi2pd/version.h:18`, `libi2pd/NetDb.hpp:54-58`); and **Java I2P**'s `net.i2p.util.VersionComparator` walks both strings to exhaustion with **no count cap**, parsing components as `long`s and treating the exhausted side as less -- the rule `i2pr` already implements. So the router is the only one of the three that refuses such a version, and that refusal is **typed** (`PublishedVersion::Uncomparable` -> `VersionEligibility::UncomparableVersion`), **counted**, **fail-closed**, and **monotone**: it can only exclude a peer both references would admit, never admit one below a floor. The **Java reading is from 2.13.1**, not the pinned **2.13.0**, and the plan says so; the primary derivation is i2pd's, whose checkout is at the exact pin. The per-component **width** divergence (the parser's `u16` bound against Java's `long`) is recorded for a successor and not fixed here. Plan 491's closure record is not rewritten  **Closed as `passed-version-component-ceiling`.** Plan 491's **F5** -- *"The comparator's four-component ceiling is an unstated assumption in the specification's terms"* -- is **discharged**. Plan 491 left it fixed in prose only, and on two grounds the plan found wrong: that *"widening the ceiling is a one-line change"* (it is compile-breaking at three sites, and it multiplies the stored array by 26 -- 10 bytes to 257 -- which multiplies the profile store's memory by about five), and that *"the plan's scope was the current table"* (the current table **is** the widest row, which is exactly the assumption F5 names). The tree held the specification's **minimum-peer obligation** rows as data -- six rows, every one of them three components -- and did **not** hold the table's feature-support rows, so the four-component row the ceiling depended on (`0.6.1.10`) lived in a prose comment. **The derivation:** `SPECIFICATION_WIDEST_VERSION_ROW` holds that row as **data**, and `MAX_API_VERSION_COMPONENTS` is that row's **width** through `version_row_width`, not a literal beside it. The stored array's width, the parser's bound, the comparison's loop bound and **the constructors' arrays** are that same constant, so a version the parser accepts is stored in full and the ordering stays **exact**. **The consequence is the point:** a future five-component row in the specification's table widens the comparator **by construction**, so such a version becomes **comparable** -- exactly what F5 says the hard-coded four denied -- instead of becoming uncomparable behind a literal. **No production behaviour changes:** the record is four components wide, so the constant is four, so the comparator accepts and refuses exactly the versions it accepts and refuses today; `ApiVersion` stays 10 bytes and `PublishedVersion` stays 12, measured rather than estimated. The plan also records, **for the first time in this repository**, what the two **pinned references** do with a version that has more components than the specification's table has ever used: **i2pd 2.61.0** (pin `635b013a612ff47278ef02acf8580a28e10e26c5`) reads `router.version` by collapsing every digit into an `int` with **no component count**, so `"0.9.62.1.2"` becomes `96212` and is **above** the floor `962` (`libi2pd/RouterInfo.cpp`, `libi2pd/version.h:18`, `libi2pd/NetDb.hpp:54-58`); and **Java I2P**'s `net.i2p.util.VersionComparator` walks both strings to exhaustion with **no cap**, parsing components as `long`s. So `i2pr` is the only one of the three that refuses such a version, and the divergence is recorded with its safety argument -- **typed** (`Uncomparable` -> `UncomparableVersion`), **counted** (`uncomparable-version`), **fail-closed**, and **monotone**, so it can only exclude a peer both references would admit and never admit one below a floor. The **Java reading is from 2.13.1**, not the pinned **2.13.0**, and is labelled as such wherever it is relied upon; the primary derivation is i2pd's, at the exact pin. The per-component **width** divergence (the parser's `u16` bound against Java's `long`) is recorded for a successor and not settled here. **The controls are not vacuous, and that is measured:** ten mutations of the real tree, each applied, run against the real lanes, and reverted byte-for-byte -- a literal ceiling fails four, a moved held row five, a **widened** held row nine, a diverged stored width four, a literal parser bound four, a truncating ordering ten, a widened floor seven, orderable published versions four, a dropped refusal counter four, and a held row narrowed below four components is refused **by the build**, because the comparator's own four-component constructor cannot fit. **The controls found a real defect in the plan's own central claim:** the constructors filled four-element array literals, so a wider row broke the build instead of widening the comparator; the constructors now build from the ceiling, the guard pins that (rule 11), and the claim is observed on the real tree rather than argued. **Advertises nothing** -- `router.version` stays `0.9.62`, `caps` and the published RouterInfo are unchanged, the new support entry **is** `experimental` with `advertised = false`, the inventory's advertised count stays **zero** (103 surfaces, 97 experimental), and `Cargo.toml` / `Cargo.lock` stay byte-identical. Plan 491's closure record is not rewritten -- the discharge lives here, in Plan 511's closure record, and in the roadmap row.|
| **512** | **closed: the destination inbound pool gets the failure detection it lacks** | [The destination inbound pool's liveness coverage](implementation/core-router-recovery/512-destination-inbound-liveness.md) -- closure `plans/closure/core-router-recovery/512-status.md` | Plan 487's **F3** -- *a controlled transit-peer loss destroys the live path; the destination inbound pool has no transit-hop failure detection* -- is recorded and unfixed: a dead transit hop is invisible to the router until the tunnel expires, so a destination whose tunnels die with a hop is not replenished until the lease expires. The mechanism the tree already has is Plan 504's **pair-level** liveness scheduler, and its **population** is the only thing that excludes the destination role -- `exploratory_liveness_pairs()` reads the exploratory pool, while the destination role's slots live in the shared data-plane registry under the structural `is_destination_role_slot` discriminator. **Every consequence of a failure is already live**: `mark_tunnel_failed` drops the tunnel from the pool, the daemon's own tick notices the changed lease set and marks the destination for republication, the replenishment deficit grows, and the existing submit path builds the replacement. The plan delivers the three missing pieces -- a destination-role population, a destination-role probe composition, and a failure arm routed to the destination -- and nothing else. The probe is addressed to the **inbound tunnel's gateway** the way **i2pd 2.61.0** does it (`libi2pd/TunnelPool.cpp:452`, at the exact pin), so the answer returns through the inbound tunnel and **no remote cooperation is required**; i2pd's *"don't fail last tunnel"* rule (`libi2pd/TunnelPool.cpp:360`) is adopted verbatim, and the **proven-pair gate** Plan 504 already established is inherited rather than relaxed, so a pair that has never answered is never torn down. The failure is a **pair-level** fact and is never written into a peer profile. The exploratory role's behaviour, guard and lane stay **byte-identical**. **Advertises nothing** -- `router.version` stays `0.9.62`, `caps` and the published RouterInfo are unchanged, and the inventory's advertised count stays zero. Plan 487's closure record is not rewritten |

### Network participation — Plans 488–520 (registered 2026-10-20)

`i2pr` is a working client-side router and a **not-yet-participating** network router. The support inventory states it exactly: of **112** surface entries, **none are advertised** (107 `experimental`, 4 `deferred`, 1 `ready`). The client-product and §6 recovery line (Plans 477–480, 485–487) closed on 2026-10-20 and is **not reopened**; the next axis is the network-participation line, owned by `plans/subsystems/network-participation-roadmap.md` and sequenced by ADR 0041.

The order is **substrate-first**, and it is not a preference. I2P's Kademlia keyspace is indexed by *floodfills*, so a **peer profile store** (capacity tiers, honest failure detection, a score, and a bounded selection policy) must precede the **floodfill-indexed routing table** with its exploration job and its **optimized, never recursive** lookup — a table of peers the router should not build through is worse than no table. The **SSU2 introducer (relay) role** follows; then the **API-version floors** that the published `router.version` attaches (the tree has no peer-version floor logic at all today); then the **transit participation posture** — all opt-in, bounded, revocable, and default-off. The **two-family qualification** (493) and the **floodfill role with the `caps=f` advertisement** (494) are registered **blocked**: 493 needs the authorized two-host topology Plan 431 stopped at, plus Plan 435's transport activation; 494 needs 493's evidence. `caps=f` is deliberately last — it is a promise about other routers' data. No support, capability, version, or advertisement change; nothing is advertised by this registration; the reference pins and the boundary scripts are untouched.
