The tree's own live test says so in a comment (`crates/i2pr-daemon/tests/netdb_tunnel_live.rs:343-344`): "the store message itself carries reply_token=0 (store-and-forget)" -- the "protocol-derived acknowledgement" it correlates is **manufactured in-process**. The reference puts the real token on the wire (`libi2pd/I2NPProtocol.cpp:272`) and, for a direct publication, a **zero** reply tunnel id meaning a direct reply (`:285`); its `PostDeliveryStatusMessage` reports "Publishing confirmed" (`libi2pd/RouterContext.cpp:1321`, `:1325`). The plan puts the token on the wire in the reference's direct-reply form, joins the live inbound path to the correlator, and distinguishes a **delivery admission** from a **network acknowledgement**. **Advertises nothing** -- `router.version` stays `0.9.62`, `caps` and the published RouterInfo are unchanged, and the inventory's advertised count stays zero. Plan 515's closure record is not rewritten and Plan 515's **F1** stays open |
| **517** | **closed: an unconfirmed publication is retried at the next closest floodfill** | [An unconfirmed publication is retried at the next closest floodfill](implementation/network-participation/517-router-info-publication-retry.md) -- closure `plans/closure/network-participation/517-status.md` | The reference does not publish once and wait 39 minutes -- it publishes, and then, while the answer has not arrived, it **asks the next closest floodfill**. The reference's cycle: `libi2pd/RouterContext.cpp:1470` clears the exclusion set and calls `Publish()`; `:1492` picks the closest eligible floodfill **not excluded** (`:1502`), sends it with a fresh reply token and **inserts it into the exclusion set** (`:1540`); `:1560` repeats that every `ROUTER_INFO_CONFIRMATION_TIMEOUT` milliseconds (`1600`, `libi2pd/RouterContext.h:37`); and `:1321` reports *"Publishing confirmed"*, clears the exclusion set and schedules the next publish at the full interval. The bound is `ROUTER_INFO_MAX_PUBLISH_EXCLUDED_FLOODFILLS` (`15`, `:38`). Plan 515 made the publication periodic and Plan 516 made it **confirmable** -- the token is on the wire and the floodfill's answer is correlated -- but a publication that is delivered and never confirmed is still unconfirmed until the next period, 39 minutes away. This plan adds the **retry ladder**: a cycle of bounded **batches**, each the closest eligible targets the cycle has not asked, with the reference's `1600` millisecond deadline between them, bounded by the reference's fifteen asked targets; a confirmation ends the cycle and a delivery refusal advances immediately. **Closed.** The publication is a **cycle**: a bounded ladder of **batches**, each batch the closest eligible targets the cycle has not already asked, so the cycle's **exclusion set** means a target is never asked twice. The **confirmation deadline** is the reference's `1600` milliseconds, measured from the moment the batch went out; a **delivery refusal** advances at once; a **confirmation ends the cycle** and clears the exclusion set; the cycle is **bounded** by the reference's fifteen asked targets; and the cycle **cannot get stuck** -- an outcome that leaves nothing to await halts it, so it never holds the next period's cycle out. The retry rides the loop's **existing** 100 millisecond tick, so no new timer is introduced, and the cycle is runtime-neutral: it takes the reading from its caller through a `PublicationClock` and never reads a clock. **Advertises nothing** -- `router.version` stays `0.9.62`, `caps` and the published RouterInfo are unchanged, and the inventory's advertised count stays zero. Plan 516's closure record is not rewritten and Plan 516's **F1** is discharged. The **stock-peer** half stays blocked on Plan 493's authorized two-host topology |
| **518** | **closed: the router's own record is re-dated and re-signed** | [The router's own record is re-dated and re-signed](implementation/network-participation/518-router-info-publication-freshness.md) -- closure `plans/closure/network-participation/518-status.md` | Predecessor: Plan 515 (the router republishes its own RouterInfo), Plan 516 (the publication asks for the network's acknowledgement), Plan 517 (an unconfirmed publication is retried at the next closest floodfill). **The gap:** the publication was periodic, confirmable and retrying, but all three re-sent the **same bytes**, and those bytes carried the publication date the router signed at **startup**. The netdb's acceptance window -- `DEFAULT_MAX_AGE`, 24 hours (`crates/i2pr-netdb/src/router_info.rs:20`) -- therefore closed on the router's own record, and the reference's netdb expires routers between one and a half and twenty-seven hours (`libi2pd/NetDb.hpp:49-51`): after a day of uptime the router kept republishing a record the network would not accept, every 39 minutes, with no counter and no diagnostic. The reference avoids this by re-dating and re-signing its record before each publish (`libi2pd/RouterInfo.cpp:1218`, `libi2pd/RouterContext.cpp:1474`). **Closed.** The router's own record now carries a **fresh** publication date. The record is re-dated and re-signed **once per cycle**, at the cycle's start, and the fresh record is **installed** as well as published, so the RouterInfo the router hands out in `SessionConfirmed` carries the fresh date too. The re-encode is the **proto's own** -- the signed region is re-encoded from the record's own decoded parts, so the addresses, the peers and the options cannot drift -- and the crypto layer **verifies its own re-sign** before returning it. The publication's **staleness rule** is runtime-neutral and uses the netdb's **own** window, so a record the network would refuse is refused and counted under its own reason (`refused_stale_record`) rather than published, and a record that cannot be re-dated is refused, never published stale. The cycle publishes **one record**: the cycle's record lives in the owner, so the first batch and every rung publish the same bytes. The router's **hash** does not change -- the key is derived from the router identity, not the signed bytes (`crates/i2pr-netdb/src/router_info.rs:249`). **Advertises nothing** -- `router.version` stays `0.9.62`, `caps` and the published RouterInfo's content are unchanged, and the inventory's advertised count stays zero. Plan 517's closure record is not rewritten, and the **M1** it carried -- the publication's delivery sharing the SSU2 service's loop -- is **closed by Plan 519**. The **stock-peer** half stays blocked on Plan 493's authorized two-host topology |
| **519** | **closed: the publication's delivery leaves the transport's loop** | [The publication's delivery leaves the transport's loop](implementation/network-participation/519-router-info-publication-off-the-transport-loop.md) -- closure `plans/closure/network-participation/519-status.md` | Plan 515 made the publication periodic, Plan 516 made it **confirmable**, Plan 517 made it **retry**, and Plan 518 made the record **current** -- but all four re-send the record from **inside the SSU2 service's `select!` loop**, and the delivery **awaits a session establishment** (`crates/i2pr-daemon/src/service_product.rs:7442` -> `crates/i2pr-daemon/src/router_i2np.rs:2438` -> `crates/i2pr-runtime/src/ssu2_runtime.rs:3480`). While that await is pending the loop cannot drain the relay queue or correlate an inbound `DeliveryStatus`. **Neither pinned reference blocks its transport's loop on a handshake**: i2pd 2.61.0's `Transports::SendMessage` posts the send and the publisher **ignores** the returned future (`libi2pd/Transports.cpp:483-496`), and Java I2P 2.13.0 runs the publication as a **job on its own pool** (`PublishLocalRouterInfoJob`) whose store is handed to a `FloodfillStoreJob` with its own `PUBLISH_TIMEOUT = 90 s` and delivered by `commSystem().processMessage(m)` -- the transport owns the dial (`StoreJob.java:415-441`). The I2P documentation states the rule: a router publishes its RouterInfo by directly connecting to a floodfill router, and only in some circumstances out an exploratory tunnel (<https://i2p.net/en/docs/overview/network-database/>). **Closed.** The publication now runs on its **own task**, with explicit ownership, explicit cancellation and its own bounded delivery timeout; the SSU2 service's loop never awaits it, the acknowledgement crosses a **bounded** channel, and the selection **prefers a peer with an established session** -- the preference both references apply. **Exploratory tunnels are out of scope**: i2pr installs none in production, so the direct route is the only one, and the batch falls back to it when no target holds a session. The publication's period stays `39 * 60` seconds: the floodfill's RouterInfo timeout is 60 minutes and the reference's own period is 39 minutes, so no cadence change is needed. **Advertises nothing** -- `router.version` stays `0.9.62` and the inventory's advertised count stays zero. **This closes the Medium finding `M1`** that Plans 517 and 518 both carried open: the publication's delivery no longer shares the transport's service loop. The **stock-peer** half is untouched and stays blocked on Plan 493's authorized two-host topology |
| **520** | **closed: the router publishes its own qualified SSU2 address** | [The router publishes its own SSU2 address](implementation/network-participation/520-router-publishes-its-own-ssu2-address.md) -- closure `plans/closure/network-participation/520-status.md` |  The plan's premise was corrected during implementation: the published record has carried one SSU2 address since the tree began, and the real gap was that the address was **unqualified** -- it omitted the `caps` and `mtu` options the form carries and the netdb's own `is_qualified_ssu2_address` demands. The plan gives the netdb a **non-floodfill addressed builder** that qualifies the address with the netdb's own rule and takes **no** floodfill permit, gives the daemon a **bounded owner** that asks the runtime for the transport's material and hands the address to that builder, makes `ssu2.advertise` a real opt-in that stays **off** by default, and relaxes the runtime's install gate so the transport's own policy -- not an ad-hoc address -- decides whether an endpoint may be published. The lapsed evidence **withdraws** the address: the published record returns to the netdb's ordinary zero-address form. **Advertises nothing** -- `router.version` stays `0.9.62`, the record's `caps` string carries no `f`, the floodfill permit remains the only route to `caps=f`, and the inventory's advertised count stays zero. The **stock-peer** half stays blocked on Plan 493's authorized two-host topology, and the direct form's **production** evidence route is recorded as the closure's open **Medium** finding `M1`
| **521** | **closed: the router runs its own peer test** | [The router runs its own peer test](implementation/network-participation/521-the-router-runs-its-own-peer-test.md) -- closure `plans/closure/network-participation/521-status.md` | Plan 520 recorded that the ordinary record's address **cannot be earned in production**: the direct form needs **three** distinct corroboration classes, and the third -- a **confirmed** peer test -- was recorded in production only by the controlled floodfill activation, which installs the *floodfill* record Plan 520's stand-down rule leaves alone. Plan 521 discharges that open **Medium** finding **M1** at its source. It gives the daemon a **bounded owner** for the I2NP peer test (message 231) the daemon already modelled and the dispatcher already classified, moves the **responder** onto the live inbound path so the inbound message is decoded **once**, and hands the runtime a **sealed** proof of a matched round trip -- a type with no constructor that takes a boolean or a claim, so the unmatched and timed-out paths cannot mint one. The runtime derives the address family from the session the exchange travelled on, so the daemon can neither assert a family nor record an unmatched exchange. The match is strict: the probed identifier **and** timestamp must match an outstanding probe **and** the answer must come from the peer the probe was sent to; the probe is recorded before the send; one probe at a time per peer; and both the probe table and the answered-key table are ceilings that refuse rather than evict. Failure is neutral -- a timeout, an unmatched echo, a peer mismatch and a refused probe all record **nothing**. `ssu2.peer_test` is an explicit opt-in, **off** by default and refused without the SSU2 service, and the owner is driven by the product's **existing** tick: no task, no timer, no listener. **Advertises nothing** -- `router.version` stays `0.9.62`, the record's `caps` string is unchanged, and the inventory's advertised count stays zero. The **stock-peer** half stays blocked on Plan 493's authorized two-host topology, and it is now blocked on a second ground as well: no stock router implements a router-level I2NP 231 responder |
| **522** | **closed: the router holds a bounded pool of outbound peer sessions** | [The router holds a bounded pool of outbound peer sessions](implementation/network-participation/522-the-router-holds-a-bounded-peer-session-pool.md) -- closure `plans/closure/network-participation/522-status.md` | The network-participation roadmap's **Transports** row names the **outbound peer-session pool** as the one local gap it still carries: *the router dials per need and holds no session between needs, so the publication's ordinary path is still the handshake path*. The arithmetic is exact: the publication runs every `39 * 60` seconds and asks **three** floodfills per batch (`crates/i2pr-daemon/src/router_info_publication.rs:72`, `:82`), while a session's idle timeout is `300_000` milliseconds (`crates/i2pr-daemon/src/config.rs:1475`) and the session terminates itself when it goes idle (`crates/i2pr-transport-ssu2/src/session.rs:2551`) -- so a session established by one publication is always gone before the next, and every batch is a fresh handshake. The router also has **no way** to hold a session open: the transport sends no keepalive of any kind, and the proto crate has no no-op I2NP message to send as one (`crates/i2pr-proto/src/i2np/message.rs:7`). The pinned reference holds sessions between needs and keeps them open with a **padding-only** keepalive that requests an immediate ACK (`libi2pd/SSU2Session.cpp:269`), and keys its live sessions by the peer's router hash (`libi2pd/SSU2.h:192`). Plan 522 delivers a **bounded outbound peer-session pool**: the transport gains an optional **outbound-only** session keepalive with its own unanswered deadline, and the daemon gains a pool that draws its peers from the router's **own** version-gated floodfill selection, dials them through the **one** existing narrow seam (`crates/i2pr-daemon/src/service_product.rs:7737`), and re-establishes a peer whose session lapsed -- on the product's **existing** tick, with no task, no timer, no listener and no socket. The **publication's delivery path is not changed**: its gate already reuses a session when one exists (`crates/i2pr-daemon/src/router_info_publication.rs:1329`), so the pool's only job is to make the session exist. `ssu2.peer_pool` is an explicit, **default-off** opt-in, so the default router's wire behaviour is unchanged. **Advertises nothing** -- `router.version` stays `0.9.62`, the published RouterInfo and `caps` are unchanged, and the inventory's advertised count stays zero. The **stock-peer** half stays blocked on Plan 493's authorized two-host topology, and the pool's **dial scope** stays bounded by the transport's own loopback/RFC1918 profile |
 | The network-participation roadmap's **Transports** row named the **outbound peer-session pool** as the one local gap it still carried, and it is now **discharged**. The router holds a **bounded pool** of outbound sessions to the peers it already knows: the population is the router's **own** version-gated floodfill selection under the **default** policy the publication's owner already holds, so there is no second policy and no second source of truth, and a peer the tree's version floor would refuse is never a candidate. The ceilings **refuse** rather than evict -- one publication cycle's worth of peers (`15`) and two dials per period -- and every refusal is counted by reason, so a router under flood cannot grow the pool past its ceiling and a reader can always tell a refused candidate from a peer the pool never tried. The pool dials through the **one** existing narrow seam (`crates/i2pr-daemon/src/service_product.rs:7744`), so the publication's delivery path is **unchanged**: its existing session check already reuses a session, and the pool's only job is to make the session exist. The pool writes **no** peer profile -- a dial outcome is this router's condition as often as the peer's -- and the dial is awaited on the pool's **own** task, for the same reason the publication has one, so the daemon's `select!` loop never blocks on a handshake. The transport gains an **outbound-only** keepalive to hold a pooled session open: a padding-only twenty-byte packet with the immediate-ACK flag set, the shape the pinned reference already sends (`libi2pd/SSU2Session.cpp:269`), carrying **no** I2NP message and adding no block type and no message type. Its unanswered deadline is its **own** -- three intervals -- because the keepalive refreshes the session's send timestamp while the idle timeout reads the **maximum** of the send and receive timestamps, so the idle timeout could never detect a dead peer. `ssu2.peer_pool` is an explicit opt-in, **off** by default, refused without the SSU2 service and refused when the session's idle timeout cannot hold the keepalive's unanswered deadline; with it off the router sends nothing it did not already send. **Advertises nothing** -- `router.version` stays `0.9.62`, the record's `caps` string is unchanged, and the inventory's advertised count stays zero. The **stock-peer** half stays blocked on Plan 493's authorized two-host topology, and the pool's **dial scope** stays bounded by the transport's own loopback/RFC1918 profile -- a transport boundary with its own plan-of-record |
| Plan 520 made the router's published SSU2 address **qualified** and gated its publication on the router's own reachability evidence, and recorded that the ordinary record's address **cannot be earned in production today**: the direct form needs `Reachable`, which the transport's tracker defines as **three distinct corroboration classes** (`crates/i2pr-transport/src/reachability.rs:66`, `:557-561`), and the runtime's production recorders can supply them only inside the **controlled floodfill activation** -- which installs the *floodfill* record, that Plan 520's stand-down rule leaves alone. That is its open **Medium** finding **M1**. **The machinery is complete and unwired**: I2NP message **231** (`crates/i2pr-proto/src/i2np/header.rs:61`) is the classic I2P peer test, the daemon already models both halves (`crates/i2pr-daemon/src/peer_test.rs:87`, `:122`, `:202`), and the central dispatcher already classifies an inbound one with the probed identifier and timestamp in hand (`crates/i2pr-daemon/src/router_i2np.rs:147`). The other two classes are **already production-reachable**: the ordinary session loop records the peer's external-address observation (`crates/i2pr-runtime/src/ssu2_runtime.rs:6097`) and the runtime records a validated path (`:5966`). The plan gives the ordinary router a **bounded production peer test** -- the responder answers on the session that delivered the test, the originator matches the echoed identifier, and a **confirmed** exchange records the router's **own** reachability -- behind an explicit, default-off opt-in. The transport's out-of-session three-party peer test stays controlled-only, and the **stock-peer** half stays blocked on Plan 493's authorized two-host topology. **Advertises nothing** -- `router.version` stays `0.9.62`, the record's `caps` string is unchanged, and the inventory's advertised count stays zero |
| **523** | **closed: the SSU2 introducer set is the specification's form** | [The SSU2 introducer set is the specification's form](implementation/network-participation/523-the-ssu2-introducer-set-is-the-specifications-form.md) -- closure `plans/closure/network-participation/523-status.md` | The SSU2 **introducer set** -- the peers that will introduce this router to others -- was the **SSU 1** form: the specification says of `ihost`/`iport`/`ikey` that those options "are for SSU only and are not used for SSU2", and the SSU2 tuple is `ih[N]` (the introducer's router hash) + `iexp[N]` (its expiry) + `itag[N]` (its relay tag) -- the form the pinned i2pd 2.61.0 publishes and parses (`libi2pd/RouterInfo.cpp:1402`, `:1413`, `:1424`; `:353`, `:359`, `:361`) and the pinned Java I2P 2.13.0 marks with `itag0` (`router/java/src/net/i2p/router/transport/udp/UDPTransport.java:2335`, `:2576`). Two defects followed, and the first was **production-reachable**: the parser refused `ih`/`iexp` as unknown options, and that refusal is reached by the daemon's dial path (`crates/i2pr-daemon/src/service_product.rs:7759`), the daemon's identity path (`crates/i2pr-daemon/src/router_i2np.rs:2108`), the floodfill service's identity (`crates/i2pr-daemon/src/floodfill.rs:238`) and three runtime sites (`crates/i2pr-runtime/src/ssu2_runtime.rs:1649`, `:3556`, `:4448`), so a peer that publishes introducers -- which a firewalled router must, and which the pinned i2pd does -- was refused outright; and the publication builder emitted the SSU 1 options the pinned i2pd reads none of, so the router's own introducer-only address, were it published, would have been unreadable. Plan 523 corrects the **form** everywhere this tree expresses one: the transport's introducer value is now `(router hash, expiry, tag)` and exposes **no** endpoint, because an SSU2 introducer is resolved by hash through the network database (`libi2pd/SSU2.cpp:986`); the parser reads `ih`/`iexp`/`itag`, **refuses** the SSU 1 options rather than ignoring them, and refuses an address carrying both a direct endpoint and introducers (`Ssu2AddressError::HostPortWithIntroducers`) because the specification forbids publishing a host or a port alongside introducers; the publication builder renders the specification's form; and the netdb gains a **second** qualification rule (`is_qualified_ssu2_introducer_address`) and a **second** builder (`build_with_ssu2_introducer_address`) that carries no floodfill permit, beside the untouched direct rule. The **composition** -- asking a peer for a relay tag, recording an introducer, publishing the router's own introducer set, and advertising the `C` capability -- is explicitly **out of scope** and is a successor's subject (Plan 524): the form must be right before it is used. **Advertises nothing** -- `router.version` stays `0.9.62`, the published RouterInfo and `caps` are unchanged, the inventory's advertised count stays **zero**, and the proto crate is untouched. Plan 490's closure record and registry row describe "the specification's introducer-only address form"; that description is **not accurate** of the tree it describes, because the form it delivered is the SSU 1 form, and the closed record is **not** rewritten -- the correction lives in this plan's closure record and in this row. Plan 520's recorded finding about the introducer-only form is **not** reopened. |
| **524** | **closed: the router asks a peer for a relay tag, and records the introducer** | [The router asks a peer for a relay tag, and records the introducer](implementation/network-participation/524-the-router-asks-a-peer-for-a-relay-tag.md) -- closure `plans/closure/network-participation/524-status.md` | The network-participation roadmap's **completion definition** requires that the router "publishes the validated introducer set in its RouterInfo address", and Plan 523 corrected the **form** of that set and left the **composition** explicitly out of scope. This plan is the **acquisition** half of the composition: the router learns that a peer is an **introducer**, asks such a peer for a **relay tag** while it is firewalled, and records the tag that comes back as an **authenticated introducer** bound to the peer that issued it. The tree held every fact it needed, and each had exactly one missing production caller: the runtime could **queue** a RelayTagRequest toward a peer and nothing queued it; the runtime **received** the answer as a `SessionEvent::RelayTag`, counted it, handed it on and dropped it, and the introducer role owner then **refused** it as `NotOurRole`; the peer-relay service owned the **insertion path** and nothing inserted; and the router could not even tell that a peer is an introducer, because `Ssu2Capabilities::supports_relay` had **no production caller**. The pinned i2pd 2.61.0 answers exactly that question from the peer's **advertised** capability (`libi2pd/RouterInfo.h:184` -- `caps & eSSUIntroducer`) and asks for a tag only when **it is firewalled** and the peer advertises it (`libi2pd/SSU2Session.cpp:753`), and this plan uses the reference's own condition. The capability is read from the peer's **validated** RouterInfo in the authoritative store and parsed with the transport's own strict parser, so a peer with no stored record, with no SSU2 address, with an unparsable address, or with an address that advertises no introducer letter is **not** an introducer, and each is a distinct counted refusal. The request rides a session the router **already holds**: the peer is drawn from the peers with a live session, no new connection is made, and **at most one** request leaves per cadence window over a **bounded**, **rotating** candidate window. The runtime keeps a **bounded** set of outstanding requests and records a tag **only** in answer to a request this router made, because a peer may send the block unsolicited and recording an introducer nobody asked for would put an unauthenticated peer into the router's introducer set. Two corrections were made to the registered design and are recorded in the plan's amendment log: **§17.2** moved the capability read from the transport's validated peer record to the daemon, because the transport's validation runs only on the **responder's** SessionConfirmed and an **outbound** session carries no peer RouterInfo at all -- the registered design could not have answered the question for the very sessions the request travels on; and **§17.3** kept the `Tag` event on the runtime's public handoff and moved the fix to the daemon's **drain**, so the role owner is no longer charged with a refusal it did not make. **§17.4** records the citation drift additively, because §3 is evidence and is not rewritten. The switch is `ssu2.introducer_request`, **off** by default and refused without the SSU2 service. The **publication** half -- building the introducer-only address, choosing it over the direct form, and signing it -- is a successor's subject, and so is the `C` capability this router publishes about **itself**; the completion definition's **publishing** half is therefore **not** discharged. **Advertises nothing** -- `router.version` stays `0.9.62`, the published RouterInfo and `caps` are unchanged, the router still advertises **no** introducer capability of its own, and the inventory's advertised count stays **zero**. Plan 523's closure record states that the router still never asks a peer for a relay tag and never records an introducer; this plan discharges the **first two** of those, and the closed record is **not** rewritten |The network-participation roadmap's **completion definition** requires that the router "publishes the validated introducer set in its RouterInfo address", and Plan 523 corrected the **form** of that set and left the **composition** explicitly out of scope. This plan is the **acquisition** half of the composition. The tree holds every fact it needs, and each fact has exactly one missing production caller: the runtime can **queue** a RelayTagRequest toward a peer (`crates/i2pr-runtime/src/ssu2_runtime.rs:2187`) and nothing queues it; the runtime **receives** the answer as a `SessionEvent::RelayTag` (`ssu2_runtime.rs:6316`), counts it, hands it on and drops it, and the introducer role owner then **refuses** it as `NotOurRole` (`crates/i2pr-daemon/src/ssu2_introducer.rs:349`) because a `Tag` is the answer to *this router's own* request, not an inbound request to serve; the peer-relay service owns the **insertion path** (`crates/i2pr-runtime/src/ssu2_peer_relay.rs:1101`) and nothing inserts; and the router cannot even tell that a peer is an introducer, because `Ssu2Capabilities::supports_relay` (`crates/i2pr-transport-ssu2/src/address.rs:472`) has no production caller. The pinned i2pd 2.61.0 answers exactly that question from the peer's **advertised** capability (`libi2pd/RouterInfo.h:184` -- `caps & eSSUIntroducer`), asks for a tag when **it is firewalled** and the peer advertises it (`libi2pd/SSU2Session.cpp:753`), mints one only if the responder is itself an introducer (`:1878-1883`), stores it (`:1889`), and **drops** it when the peer's RouterInfo stops advertising the capability (`:1990-1991`). The plan makes the transport's **validated** peer record carry the peer's advertised capabilities, gives the runtime the answer for a peer it holds a session with, makes the daemon ask exactly one eligible peer per cadence window when the router is firewalled, and makes the runtime record the tag that arrives as an **authenticated introducer**. The **publication** half -- building the introducer-only address, choosing it over the direct form, and signing it -- stays out of scope, and the `C` capability this router publishes about **itself** is a third, separate matter. **Advertises nothing** -- `router.version` stays `0.9.62`, the published RouterInfo and `caps` are unchanged, and the inventory's advertised count stays **zero** |
| **525** | **closed: the router publishes the introducer set it learned** | [The router publishes the introducer set it learned](implementation/network-participation/525-the-router-publishes-the-introducer-set-it-learned.md) -- closure `plans/closure/network-participation/525-status.md` | The network-participation roadmap's **completion definition** requires that the router "publishes the validated introducer set in its RouterInfo address", Plan 523 corrected the **form** of that set, and Plan 524 delivered the **acquisition** half. This plan is the **publication** half: the router builds the specification's introducer-only address from the introducer set it learned, chooses it over the direct form when the direct form is not available, and signs it through the netdb's own **second** qualification rule. The tree held every fact it needed, and each fact had exactly one missing production caller: the runtime's `introducer_publication_material` had none, the netdb's `build_with_ssu2_introducer_address` and `is_qualified_ssu2_introducer_address` had none, and the daemon's self-publication path asked the runtime for the **direct** form only. **The registration also records a latent defect in the very function the plan needs**: `introducer_publication_material` cannot succeed, because it passes the same second for the build time and the evidence expiry (`crates/i2pr-runtime/src/ssu2_runtime.rs:2612`, `:2613`) and the transport's tested gate (`crates/i2pr-transport-ssu2/src/publication.rs:239`, `:544`) therefore always withdraws the snapshot, so the function always fails with `InvalidPublication` -- a refusal that names a malformed address when the real cause is a withdrawn snapshot. It is invisible today only because the function has no production caller. The defect is a **failing gate**, not a missing one: the transport's gate is correct and the caller passes the wrong value into it, so the fix is in the caller. Two decisions are explicit: the **composition** lives in the runtime and the daemon branches on a **typed form** (`Direct` / `IntroducerOnly`) rather than on a refusal code, because the direct form's availability and the introducer set's liveness are the runtime's facts; and the family capability is derived from the router's **bound** socket rather than hardcoded to `"4"`, because the current builder makes a factual claim about a router that may have no IPv4 socket at all. The daemon's counters distinguish the two published forms and the withdrawal, the empty-introducer-set reason becomes **reachable** (its counter was permanently zero), and the counters become readable through the existing inspection seam. No configuration key is added: `ssu2.advertise` already means "the router publishes its own SSU2 address", and the introducer-only form is that address in its other shape. The **`C` advertisement** this router publishes about **itself** is a third, separate matter and stays out of scope, as does the withdrawal form and the **stock-peer** half, which stays blocked on Plan 493's authorized two-host topology. **Advertises nothing** -- `router.version` stays `0.9.62`, the published RouterInfo and `caps` are unchanged, and the inventory's advertised count stays **zero**. **Plan 525 is closed** as `passed-router-publishes-the-introducer-set-it-learned`; record `plans/closure/network-participation/525-status.md`. The **publication** half of the composition is **delivered**: the router publishes the validated introducer set it learned, in the specification's introducer-only address form -- the reference's unpublished SSU2 shape, option for option (`libi2pd/RouterInfo.cpp:1350`-`:1466`). The **runtime** owns the decision and the daemon matches on a **typed** form, so the daemon holds no copy of the publication rule; the netdb's **second** rule and **second** builder get their first production caller. Two latent defects were fixed on the way, both invisible until now because the path had no production caller: the runtime's introducer-only builder could **never succeed**, because it passed the build time as the snapshot's evidence expiry and the transport's own tested gate therefore withdrew every snapshot; and the runtime's install gate could not admit the form at all, because it required every SSU2 address to name the router's own bound **endpoint** and the introducer-only form has none -- the gate now binds the router's own identity and contact material **by the address's form**. The snapshot's evidence expiry is the **earliest** expiry among the published introducers, and the family capability is derived from the router's **bound** socket rather than hardcoded. The daemon's counters **distinguish** the two published forms and the withdrawal, the empty-introducer-set reason becomes **reachable** (its counter was permanently zero), and the counters become readable through the existing inspection seam. No configuration key is added: `ssu2.advertise` already means "the router publishes its own SSU2 address", and the introducer-only form is that address in its other shape. The **withdrawal** is unchanged -- a firewalled router with no introducer still publishes the netdb's ordinary zero-address record -- and the **`C` advertisement** remains a **third**, separate matter with its own plan-of-record. The **stock-peer** half stays blocked on Plan 493's authorized two-host topology. **Advertises nothing** -- `router.version` stays `0.9.62`, the published RouterInfo and `caps` are unchanged, and the inventory's advertised count stays **zero** |
| **526** | **closed: the router advertises its own introducer role** | [The router advertises its own introducer role](implementation/network-participation/526-the-router-advertises-its-own-introducer-role.md) -- closure `plans/closure/network-participation/526-status.md` | The network-participation roadmap's **completion definition** requires that the router "publishes the validated introducer set in its RouterInfo address". Plan 523 corrected the **form** of that set, Plan 524 delivered the **acquisition** and Plan 525 the **publication**; this plan delivers the **advertisement** -- the specification's Relay capability, the `"C"` letter, in the `caps` option of the router's **own** address. The tree held every fact the plan needed: the transport already modelled the `C` letter (`Ssu2Capabilities::RELAY`) and parsed a family-and-role combination per character, and the runtime already knew whether the introducer role was live (`introducer_service_enabled`). What was missing was the **letter** and a **rule** to carry it -- the netdb's direct rule demands the capability string be exactly the address's own family, so a role-bearing address could never be qualified or signed. **The registration also records a latent defect in Plan 499's live path**: the daemon's composition refused the SSU2 service outright whenever `ssu2.introducer_service` was set, so the role owner the composition constructs was **unreachable in every configuration**, and Plan 499's closed claim that "the daemon's live loop then constructs the role owner and the carriage" was false of the tree. The blanket refusal is replaced by the role's own explicit, default-off opt-in, so the advertisement can be truthful. The netdb gains a **third** qualification rule and a **third** builder, beside the untouched direct and introducer rules; the third rule is **exact** -- the address's own family letter followed by the role letter -- so `"BC"`, `"4CB"` and `"46C"` are refused and an unearned role cannot reach the wire, and the third builder carries no floodfill permit, matching the reference, which clears the role for a floodfill (`libi2pd/RouterContext.cpp:655`). The **runtime** owns the composition of the letter and the wire, so the letter rides the **direct** form only -- the introducer-only form publishes no endpoint, so it is not an introducer to a reference peer (`libi2pd/RouterInfo.cpp:1059`) -- and it is published **only** while the role is live. A stale letter is **refused**, not omitted: the runtime's install gate refuses a role-bearing record while the role is not live, so the installed record cannot stand with a claim the router has stopped making. The daemon matches on a **typed** published form, hands each form to the builder that carries its own rule, and counts the three published forms -- the direct form, the **role-bearing** direct form and the introducer-only form -- separately, with the third rule's refusal under its own reason. The tree publishes the **combination** `"4C"` rather than the reference's role-only `"C"`, keeping the tree's stricter family requirement; both are inside the specification's `[B,C,4,6]` alphabet and both references parse a combination. The readiness contract's control is **repinned**, not dropped: the invariant it guarded still holds and its enforcement point moves to the install gate, and a second control pins the opt-in; the mutation self-test still proves every control has teeth. Plan 520's and Plan 525's guards are **extended**, not weakened: each extended rule names the new location as well as the old one and fails closed on an absent item. The **`B` advertisement**, the **`caps=f` advertisement** and the **stock-peer** half stay out of scope, with their owners named. **Advertises nothing** -- `router.version` stays `0.9.62`, the new surface is `experimental` with `advertised = false`, the inventory's advertised count stays **zero** and `Cargo.toml` and `Cargo.lock` stay byte-identical. **Plan 526 is closed** as `passed-router-advertises-its-own-introducer-role`; record `plans/closure/network-participation/526-status.md`. With this plan the SSU2 introducer composition is **complete on the router's side**: the router learns that a peer is an introducer, asks it for a relay tag, publishes the introducers it learned, and advertises that it is itself an introducer. The **stock-peer** half of every one of those remains blocked on Plan 493's authorized two-host topology, and the `caps=f` advertisement remains Plan 494's |
| **527** | **closed: the router's reachability has one owner and one reader** | [The router's reachability has one owner and one reader](implementation/network-participation/527-the-routers-reachability-has-one-owner-and-one-reader.md) -- closure `plans/closure/network-participation/527-status.md` | The router's reachability had **two** trackers, and the product read the one that only ever received **confirming** evidence. The runtime owned one, inside its service state (`crates/i2pr-runtime/src/ssu2_runtime.rs:1371`), and the peer-relay service owned another with the **default** policy rather than the runtime's. Every **contradicting** signal was written only into the second: `RelayFirewalledSignal` at exactly one place, `AddressMismatch` and `FirewalledLikely` at exactly two. The tracker the product read received only `LocalConfiguredBind`, `PeerTestResult{Confirmed}`, `ValidatedPath` and `AuthenticatedPeerObservedExternalAddress`, **none** of which contradicts -- so `contradicting_classes()` was permanently 0, `ReachabilityState::Firewalled` and `Unreachable` were unreachable states in the running router, and the peer-relay service's snapshot had **no reader in the daemon at all**. The consequence reached the whole SSU2 introducer composition: the daemon's firewalled gate reads the runtime's tracker, so it was **permanently false**, the request owner always refused with `NotFirewalled`, the router **never** asked a peer for a relay tag, never obtained an authenticated introducer, and the introducer set it publishes stayed empty -- leaving Plan 525's publication with nothing to publish and Plan 526's `"C"` advertisement riding a role whose acquisition half could never run in the field. Plan 524's lane drove the owner with the flag **injected** (`crates/i2pr-daemon/tests/ssu2_introducer_request.rs:498`), so it proved the consumer honours the flag and never exercised the producer. The second defect was that the transport **decoded** the data-phase Address block, which carries a validated endpoint, and then **discarded** it -- the event was a unit variant, so the router was told that a peer observed an external address but never **which** address. This plan removes the second tracker: the peer-relay service keeps its counters and **returns** the typed facts it computes, and the **runtime** records them. Every typed peer-test outcome is recorded, not only the confirming one -- recording only the confirming kind is precisely what kept the tracker from ever being contradicted -- and the runtime owns the relay-success recorder as well, because the transport's own state machine needs **two distinct contradicting classes** before it calls the router firewalled. The transport keeps the endpoint it already decoded (**no codec, block or option changes**), and the runtime compares the observation with the router's **own bound socket**: an agreement corroborates, a disagreement contradicts, and a family with no bound socket refuses the comparison. One observation is one fact, so a contradiction never also corroborates. The transport's reachability **model** -- the five signal kinds, the corroboration classes, the policy floor and the state machine -- is untouched. Plan 524's closed claim about the firewalled state is corrected in Plan 527's closure record and here, never in Plan 524's own record: the claim was **true of the consumer and false of the producer**, because nothing in the tree could ever *earn* the state. Plan 521's peer-test guard is **extended**, not weakened: the original branch is kept, the delegation branch is checked rather than assumed, the absent-recorder case fails closed, and two inverted probes are added. The **requester** side of the SSU2 relay protocol stays unwired, the **`B` advertisement** and the **`caps=f` advertisement** stay out of scope, and the **stock-peer** half stays blocked on Plan 493's authorized two-host topology. **Advertises nothing** -- `router.version` stays `0.9.62`, the new surface is `experimental` with `advertised = false`, the inventory's advertised count stays **zero** and `Cargo.toml` and `Cargo.lock` stay byte-identical. **Plan 527 is closed** as `passed-the-router-s-reachability-has-one-owner-and-one-reader`; record `plans/closure/network-participation/527-status.md`. |
| **528** | **closed: the router's peer-test verdict must not be thrown away** | [The router's peer-test verdict must not be thrown away](implementation/network-participation/528-the-routers-peer-test-verdict-must-not-be-thrown-away.md) -- closure `plans/closure/network-participation/528-status.md` | The tree ran a **complete** three-party SSU2 peer test whose verdict carried exactly what the third party observed, and then threw every verdict away except "the observation matched the router's own bound address and two peers corroborated": a **disagreement** became `ExchangeNotConfirmed`, an **error**, not a **fact**. The specification's peer-test result table has four outcomes -- UNKNOWN, OK, FIREWALLED, SYMNAT -- and the pinned i2pd 2.61.0 concludes **FIREWALLED from a single peer test** (`libi2pd/SSU2Session.cpp:2703`-`:2705`); the tree's `ControlledPeerTestOutcome` had exactly one variant, and it was `Confirmed`. The transport's state machine compounded it: the **corroboration** floor of 2 was applied to **contradictions**, and every peer-test verdict was filed under **one** corroboration class, so two peer-test verdicts contradicted **once** and the contradiction floor was never reached -- one contradiction left the router on `ObservedUnconfirmed` for ever, and `Firewalled` and `Unreachable` were **unreachable states**: the daemon's firewalled gate was **permanently false**, and with it the SSU2 introducer composition's acquisition half. Plan 528 makes the verdict a **fact**: the transport's **contradiction** floor is separated from its corroboration floor, so one contradicting fact counts -- the reference's own single-test rule -- while the corroboration floor stays 2 and `Reachable` still needs three distinct supporting classes, and the **positive** claim is not weakened at all. The **firewalled verdict** is first class: a peer test that concludes the router is not directly reachable, and the relay-firewalled indication, put the router in `Firewalled`, and the runtime's comparison against the router's **own bound socket** records a disagreement as that verdict, because a peer seeing the router at an address it does not own **is** the router's firewalled fact -- the same fact the specification's FIREWALLED row reports, so both routes reach the same state. The controlled driver's outcome enum grew the verdicts it used to throw away, so a mismatch is returned as a fact and its error arm keeps only the genuinely **failed** cases. The specification's peer-test result table is transcribed into the tree as data and pinned. **Corrections to the closed plans' claims:** Plan 527's invariant 9 said the transport's reachability model was byte-identical -- the five signal kinds and the corroboration classes still are, but the policy now carries a second floor and the `Firewalled` branch asks a new question; Plan 527's lane and guard, and Plan 160's pinned model test, asserted a state this plan's model correction supersedes. None of those closed records is rewritten; the corrections live in this row, in the closure record and in the plan's amendment log. The **production** peer-test driver, the **requester** side, the **`B` advertisement**, the **`caps=f` advertisement** and the **stock-peer** half stay out of scope, with their owners named. **Advertises nothing** -- `router.version` stays `0.9.62`, the new surface is `experimental` with `advertised = false`, the inventory's advertised count stays **zero** and `Cargo.toml` and `Cargo.lock` stay byte-identical. |
| **529** | **closed: the router must learn the address at which a peer sees it** | [The router must learn the address at which a peer sees it](implementation/network-participation/529-the-router-must-learn-the-address-at-which-a-peer-sees-it.md) -- closure `plans/closure/network-participation/529-status.md` | The SSU2 specification's Address block carries **the address at which the recipient sees the sender**, and the tree both sent it with the wrong value and threw the answer away. The responder put its **own** bound address in the Session Created's Address block, where the specification says the **initiator's** observed address belongs and where the pinned i2pd 2.61.0 puts `m_RemoteEndpoint` (`libi2pd/SSU2Session.cpp:999`); and the initiator decrypted the Session Created's payload and took only the establishment token from it, never reading the Address block, while the reference processes the very same payload (`libi2pd/SSU2Session.cpp:1110`) and derives **"Our external address is "** from it (`:2072`). The **receiver's** seam already existed -- Plan 527 made the runtime compare an observed address with the router's own bound socket -- but the handshake never reached it, so `AuthenticatedPeerObservedExternalAddress`, corroboration **class 1**, had **no field producer** and `Reachable` could not be earned: Plan 526's recorded open limitation, still open after Plans 527 and 528. The responder's Address block now carries the **observed source address** of the request it answers, taken from the datagram's own header and never from a configured or bound value; `ResponderConfig::local_address` is gone, because the value it held was never the right one. The initiator **reads** the block out of the decrypted Session Created payload, and the responder reads the Session Request's block in the transition that decrypts the request and carries it across to the transition that establishes the session. The direction is symmetric: the tree also gains Alice's view of Bob in the Session Request, which it never sent, because the specification's block is symmetric and the reference reads it in both directions (`libi2pd/SSU2Session.cpp:934`). The runtime routes the observation into Plan 527's comparison, so an agreement corroborates and a disagreement is the router's own firewalled verdict under Plan 528's model; the comparison is recorded only once a session was really established, so a corroboration class is never counted for a session that does not exist. A missing or malformed Address block leaves the session **established** and records nothing: the observation is evidence, not a handshake requirement. **No wire format changes** -- the same block, the same type, the same length; only the value changes, to what the specification and the reference already use. **Corrections to the closed plans' claims:** Plan 161's closure record named this exact defect and deferred it as follow-up work, and this plan is that follow-up work; Plan 282's closure record rejected recording handshake Address blocks as observations, and that objection was sound against the tree as it stood, because the block carried the **sender's own self-report** -- this plan changes the block's value, which is what the objection was about. None of those closed records is rewritten; the corrections live in this row, in the closure record and in the plan's amendment log. The **production** peer-test driver, the **requester** side, the **`B` advertisement**, the **`caps=f` advertisement** and the **stock-peer** half stay out of scope, with their owners named. **Advertises nothing** -- `router.version` stays `0.9.62`, the new surface is `experimental` with `advertised = false`, the inventory's advertised count stays **zero** and `Cargo.toml` and `Cargo.lock` stay byte-identical. |
| **505** | **closed** | [The exploratory build driver in the product composition](implementation/network-participation/505-exploratory-build-driver-in-the-product-composition.md) | Predecessor: Plan 185 (the one-hop exploratory tunnels and the liveness scheduler), Plan 186 (the NetDB lookup / LeaseSet2 publication), Plan 488 (the peer-profile store), Plan 491 (the API-version floors and peer eligibility), Plan 504 (the liveness scheduler in the product composition). **The gap: the router never names a peer to build a first exploratory tunnel with.** Plan 185 delivered the mechanism — the build coordinator, the one-hop install path and the liveness scheduler — and Plan 504 installed the liveness scheduler in the live product with an authenticated correlation and a proven-pair gate. But the **population** the scheduler watches does not exist: `ExploratoryBuildCoordinator::submit` (`crates/i2pr-daemon/src/exploratory_build.rs:1599`) has **zero production callers** — every call site is an out-of-crate integration lane — so the daemon installs no exploratory tunnels, `exploratory_liveness_pairs()` returns empty, and the scheduler tracks **zero pairs**: installed and inert. The production rebuild path (`crates/i2pr-daemon/src/service_product.rs:3337` → `coordinator.rebuild_exploratory(...)` at `:3376`) is complete but unreachable, because no pair exists to be removed. **The mechanism is complete; only its inputs are missing.** `rebuild_exploratory` (`crates/i2pr-daemon/src/exploratory_build.rs:2679`) is already a complete first-build driver with the peer-selection step removed, and `install_established` (`:2469`) already installs an exploratory build into the pool and the registry. **One new thing is needed: a peer.** The peer-profile store is constructed (`crates/i2pr-daemon/src/lib.rs:1902`) but **never fed** — `learn` (`crates/i2pr-daemon/src/netdb_participation.rs:262`) has one caller and it is inside `#[cfg(test)]` (`lib.rs:5911`) — and `select_tunnel_build_peers` (`netdb_participation.rs:384`), which is Plan 491's version-gated selection with `require_floodfill: false`, has **no** production caller. The four `observe_*` calls have no production caller either, so the store is empty in a live router. **Plan 505's driver is the component that produces exactly those signals.** Delivered: a driver that names a peer through the version-gated selection, builds through the **single** existing exploratory build path, installs the result, and records **exactly one** observation per terminal outcome into the profile store. It runs on the **existing** 250 ms group tick beside `drive_tunnel_liveness`, behind an operator opt-in that is **off by default** (the `[transit] participation` precedent, `crates/i2pr-daemon/src/config.rs:957`). **The scope is bounded by the transport, and the plan does not widen it:** `Ssu2DialTarget::new` (`crates/i2pr-runtime/src/ssu2_runtime.rs:647`) is loopback-only and `new_private_lan` (`:679`) accepts only RFC1918 IPv4, so a driver can only build toward a peer it can already reach or may legally dial. A public-internet dial scope would be a transport-boundary change with its own plan-of-record. **Plan 185's closure record needs no correction** — it delivered the mechanism and never claimed the production daemon initiates exploratory builds, so this is an open gap rather than a false claim; the Plan 503/504 precedent does not apply. **Not closed here:** the validated store's refresh from live NetDB lookups (RouterInfos learned from a live lookup are still not inserted into it); Plan 497 limitation 5's **read** half (the driver becomes a live reader of the profile store, but the routing table's only reader remains Plan 433's never-constructed `NetDbTunnelCoordinator`); the **stock-peer question** — whether an ordinary public router accepts a driver-initiated one-hop exploratory build, and whether it relays a liveness probe back, is unproven; Plan 487 F3's destination-inbound half; Plan 491 F2 and F4; Plan 490 Medium #3; Plan 502 M1; and Plan 503's lookup-target API-version gate. **Advertised nothing** — `router.version` stays `0.9.62`, `caps` and the published RouterInfo are unchanged, the new support entry will be `experimental` with `advertised = false`, the inventory's advertised count stays **zero**, and `Cargo.toml`/`Cargo.lock` stay byte-identical **Plan 505 is closed** as `passed-exploratory-build-driver-in-the-product-composition`; record `plans/closure/network-participation/505-status.md`, implementation `2ea35299` (+ `774312f5`, `098d9b8e`, `705a6ea7`, `e9c0bdd8`; the plan corrections `b2222746` and `d8eaede2` are committed separately, as Plan 504 did). Predecessor: Plan 185 (the one-hop exploratory tunnels and the liveness scheduler), Plan 186 (the NetDB lookup / LeaseSet2 publication), Plan 488 (the peer-profile store), Plan 491 (the API-version floors and peer eligibility), Plan 504 (the liveness scheduler in the product composition). **The mechanism was complete; only its inputs were missing.** `ExploratoryBuildCoordinator::submit` had **zero production callers** -- every call site was an out-of-crate lane -- so the daemon installed no exploratory tunnels, the production rebuild path was unreachable (no pair existed to be removed), and the peer-profile store -- constructed in production -- was **never fed**: `learn` had one caller and it was inside `#[cfg(test)]`, `select_tunnel_build_peers` had none at all, and the four `observe_*` calls had none either. `rebuild_exploratory` was already a complete first-build driver with the peer-selection step removed, and `install_established` already installed an exploratory build into the pool and the registry. **One new thing was needed: a peer.** Delivered: a driver that names a peer through **Plan 491's version-gated selection** (`require_floodfill: false` -- an exploratory tunnel is built toward an ordinary peer, not necessarily a floodfill) and refuses a below-floor, unknown-version or uncomparable-version peer **by reason**, never silently; that builds through the **existing** `rebuild_exploratory`, so there is exactly **one** exploratory build path and the first build and the liveness rebuild cannot drift apart; that installs the result into the pool and the registry; and that records exactly one peer-level observation per terminal outcome. The peer's static encryption key is the **RouterInfo identity public key** -- the production derivation -- and **not** the SSU2 address `s` option that Plan 185's external lane found to be the wrong key; the selection and the peer-material resolver read the **same** store, so they can never disagree about which peer exists. **The outcome map is total over all fourteen `BuildCoordinatorOutcome` variants and it separates a peer fault from a local condition.** `Installed` answers, `TimedOut` is unanswered, `HopRejected` is refused, `DeliveryFailed` is unreachable and `InvalidReply` is misbehaving -- but `Cancelled`, `PoolRejected`, `InvalidRegistration`, `InvalidPath` and `CoordinatorRejected` are **this router's** condition and produce **no** peer-profile write: the router must never blame a peer for a fault of its own, and writing a local failure into a peer's profile would degrade a good peer's score. **The plan's own table was wrong here, and was corrected before the code followed it** (`d8eaede2`); no earlier closure record was rewritten. An outcome with no in-flight attempt, a wrong direction or a mismatched attempt id is an **orphan**: counted and credited to nothing. **The peer-profile store is now fed in production.** The daemon learns its real validated peers from the bootstrap snapshot -- the same population the routing table is populated from -- with **one** receipt per batch, and the snapshot is taken under the bootstrap's lock while the batch is learned outside it, so the two locks are never held at once. A learned peer does not reset its restored observation state. **The posture is off by default**: a new `[exploratory]` section carries one switch, `builds`, following the `[transit] participation` precedent, and it is `deny_unknown_fields` so a key that looks like an advertisement is refused rather than ignored. There is deliberately **no** count knob -- the pair count is bounded by the coordinator's pool and Plan 504's `MAX_LIVENESS_PAIRS`, and a key that could disagree with those ceilings would be a second source of truth. Off by default for a concrete reason, not out of caution: the SSU2 dial target is loopback-only and the private-LAN variant accepts only RFC1918 IPv4, so a public deployment's peer population is **not dialable at all**. **No new task, no new timer, no new listener, no new socket, no new dependency** -- the driver runs on the **existing** 250 ms group tick beside Plan 504's liveness driver, and the terminal outcomes reach it on **both** the expiry sweep and the inbound build-reply path. **The feasibility answer is measured and the artefact is not vacuous.** It drives a controlled mesh of two real SSU2 daemons against the real coordinator, profile store and delivery seam; swapping the responder's identity encryption key for the SSU2 static key -- the shortcut the pre-existing harness uses on both sides -- makes the build fail to install. The guard was likewise verified non-vacuous against the real tree: dropping a variant from the outcome mapping makes rule 6 fail, and removing the expiry sweep's observation call makes rule 9 fail. **Plan 497 limitation 5 is partially discharged**: the store is no longer empty in a live router, and the driver is a live writer of the four observation signals and a live reader for its selection -- but the **routing table's** only reader remains Plan 433's never-constructed `NetDbTunnelCoordinator`, so that half stays open and this plan does not pretend otherwise. **Plan 185's closure record needs no correction** -- it delivered the mechanism and never claimed the production daemon initiates exploratory builds, so this is an open gap rather than a false claim, and the Plan 503/504 precedent does not apply. **Advertised nothing** -- `router.version` stays `0.9.62`, `caps` and the published RouterInfo are unchanged, the new support entry is `experimental` with `advertised = false`, the inventory's advertised count stays **zero**, and `Cargo.toml`/`Cargo.lock` stay byte-identical. **Not closed here:** the validated store's refresh from live NetDB lookups (the driver's population is the bootstrap snapshot); Plan 487 F3's **destination-inbound** half (it needs tunnel rekeying, which the tree does not implement at all); Plan 497 limitation 5's read half; Plan 487 F2; Plan 491 F2 and F4; Plan 490 Medium #3; Plan 502 M1; Plan 503's lookup-target API-version gate; and **no stock-peer evidence** -- whether an ordinary public router accepts a driver-initiated one-hop exploratory build, and whether it relays a liveness probe back, is unproven. **The next candidate on this line is the validated store's refresh from live NetDB lookups**: it is the population the driver draws from, and it needs its own plan-of-record because it changes what the router stores. |

| **532** | **closed: the router must answer a relay introduction** | [The router must answer a relay introduction](implementation/network-participation/532-the-router-must-answer-a-relay-introduction.md) -- closure `plans/closure/network-participation/532-status.md` | The SSU2 relay protocol has three roles. The **target** was the last role with no producer. The transport's target state machine was **complete** -- `RelayTarget` verifies the introducer context against the specification's preimage, enforces the global quota, suppresses a replayed `(nonce, alice_hash)` pair and expires pending introductions deterministically -- and the runtime already held the table and exposed the seam that drives it, but that seam had **zero production callers**: its only caller was Plan 531's own lane. The introduction was delivered to the **wrong** role and refused: the session loop routed a Relay Intro to the introducer's handoff unconditionally, and the introducer owner refused it as `NotOurRole`, so the target's transport table was never fed. The target's **outbound** half had no producer either: nothing emitted the accept and nothing emitted the hole punch outside Plan 531's lane. The plan composes what the tree already owns. The **policy** is the daemon's (`crates/i2pr-daemon/src/relay_target.rs`): it reads the requester's **validated** RouterInfo out of the authoritative store, takes the requester's signing key and introduction key from that validated record through the transport's strict `Ssu2RouterAddress::parse`, drives the runtime's existing `on_target_relay_intro` seam so the transport's own state machine is used and not re-implemented, signs the accept with the router's **own** identity key over the introducer-bound preimage `relay_response_preimage(bob_hash, ...)`, and hands the hole punch's pieces to the runtime's outbound seam. The **mechanics** stay in the runtime, which owns the sockets, the session, the bounded tables and the transport's verification. The hole punch is **staged, never sessioned**: it is sealed under the *requester's* introduction key, built with the transport's own codec, staged through the runtime's existing bounded queue as control traffic, and its destination is checked against the **union** of the two dial profiles the runtime offers, so the target role cannot widen the router's reach. The transport's relay state machines are **used, not edited** -- the plan changes **no transport file at all**. The daemon records **no** reachability signal of its own: a router that answers an introduction is reachable **through the relay**, not directly, so the answer gains **no** corroborating class and the transport's class-4 contradiction stays class 4. Two honest limits are recorded, not hidden: the **requester's establishment dial** that would consume the accept's token is not in this tree, and the requester owner has no deferred-punch buffer, so the transport's own requester table refuses a hole punch that arrives before the accept -- the lane holds one punch aside until the accept is correlated. The delivery stays confined to the **controlled loopback profile**, the **production** SSU2 peer-test driver stays unbuilt, the `B` advertisement and `caps=f` stay with their owners, and the **stock-peer** half stays blocked on Plan 493's authorized two-host topology. **Advertises nothing** -- `router.version` stays `0.9.62` and the inventory's advertised count stays zero |

The exchange was already complete in **shape** -- Plan 531 gave the requester its role and Plan 532 gave the target's -- but it ended at a hole punch, and three gaps stood in the way. | **535** | **closed: the router must keep its own bound address counted as reachability evidence** | [the router must keep its own bound address counted as reachability evidence](implementation/network-participation/535-the-router-must-keep-its-own-bound-address-counted-as-reachability-evidence.md) -- closure `plans/closure/network-participation/535-status.md` | The router's own class **stays** counted. The transport's reachability model needs **three distinct** supporting classes before it will say `Reachable`, and the explicit bind -- Plan 530's class 0 -- is the only one of the three that is a fact about **this router** rather than about a peer. Two of them are refreshed by their peers: the peer's observed external address (Plan 529) arrives when a peer sees the router, and the confirmed peer test (Plan 521) arrives when a peer answers the router's test. The third was refreshed by **nobody**: it was recorded once, at bind time, and the model holds every signal as a timed observation, so after the thirty-minute observation lifetime the class **lapsed** and was gone for ever -- the runtime polls the expiry on each of the paths that read the evidence, so the lapse was not theoretical: a long-running router silently lost the one class it could have kept, its corroboration count could never exceed two again, and `Reachable` was unreachable until the next restart. Plan 535 makes the router **re-assert** its own standing fact, and the router now does. The rule is public and pure (`explicit_bind_refresh_due`): the re-assertion is due once the record has aged past **half** the observation lifetime -- strictly before the record can lapse, so a read between an expiry and the next pass can never find the class already gone -- and it **fails closed**, because a class that is re-asserted too often is harmless and a class that lapses is not. The re-assertion is gated on the **same** existing opt-in the recorder is, so a runtime that did not ask for explicit-bind corroboration re-asserts nothing; it reads the observation lifetime from the **tracker's own policy**, so the expiry rule keeps its **one** owner and the runtime cannot drift from it; and it rides the runtime's **existing** maintenance pass, so **no new timer** is introduced. The families the router's own sockets can be named for are captured at bind time, because the runtime binds once and does not rebind, so the re-assertion needs neither the sockets lock nor a second clock and the lock order is never inverted. The re-assertion touches the router's **own** tracker and nothing else: it writes no peer profile, claims no corroborating class for any peer, and records no reachability signal about anything but this router's own socket. Every re-assertion is counted under its own counter, and the counter carries counts only. The transport's reachability **model** is untouched: its signal vocabulary, its corroboration classes, its floors, its states and its expiry semantics are exactly what the tree already had, and the tracker gains no method. **No configuration key is added**: the daemon's configuration surface is unchanged, and the daemon's mapping names the runtime's own default policy. Plan 530's recorded limitation -- the class lapsing with the observation TTL -- is **discharged**; the correction lives in this row and in Plan 535's closure record, and **Plan 530's closed record is not rewritten**. The adjustment is checked in both directions and refuses an impossible adjustment rather than wrapping it, `stats()` reads the maintained counter and walks no record map, and the bootstrap computes the statistics **once** per operation because the refresh takes the statistics it refreshes. | **541** | **closed: the router's floodfill selection must not be narrowed by the group provider's bound** | [the router's floodfill selection must not be narrowed by the group provider's bound](implementation/network-participation/541-the-routers-floodfill-selection-must-not-be-narrowed-by-the-group-providers-bound.md) -- closure `plans/closure/network-participation/541-status.md` | The router's **authoritative** floodfill selection store -- the `DestinationTunnelCoordinator`'s store, which `candidate_hashes` and `version_gated_candidate_hashes` read -- was fed `Bootstrap::validated_router_info_snapshot()`, and that snapshot is the **first 256 records in ascending `RouterHash` order**. | **542** | **closed: the NetDB's retention must not depend on the service-tunnel posture** | [the NetDB's retention must not depend on the service-tunnel posture](implementation/network-participation/542-the-netdbs-retention-must-not-depend-on-the-service-tunnel-posture.md) -- closure `plans/closure/network-participation/542-status.md` | `RouterInfoStore::insert` **refuses** at the quota and **never evicts**, and the tree's own documentation of the prune states the consequence in as many words: a NetDB that filled up would refuse every new peer, permanently, and would keep a record the router's own validation policy would refuse to accept today. The operation that prevents that -- `Bootstrap::prune` -- had exactly **one** production caller, `PeerReconcile::prune_bootstrap`, and `PeerReconcile` is constructed **only** inside `ServiceProduct`, whose only `ServiceProductSpec` was built inside `if has_enabled_groups`. That gate was `service_tunnels_active(config)` -- I2PControl, router-network SAM, or an enabled service tunnel -- and every one of those is **false** by default. So in the posture where the router ran SSU2 and nothing else, the NetDB was **write-only**: the reseed, the NetDB cache and the authenticated unsolicited `DatabaseStore` kept feeding it, it reached `netdb.max_records` (4 096) or `netdb.max_encoded_bytes` (4 MiB), and then it refused **every** subsequent peer for as long as the router ran. The retention prune was not narrowed; it was **unreachable**. The plan gave the retention prune an owner that runs whenever the NetDB is enabled, so the router's peer knowledge turns over in every posture. It **shares** the retention window rather than restating it -- Plan 507's closed guard pins its derivation, so the new caller **imports** it -- and it reuses the reconcile's own `PruneOutcome`, which became `pub`, so the two callers cannot disagree about what a prune outcome is. The step rides the **participation maintenance period** the router already configures, so **no** new task, timer, listener, socket or configuration key is added, and the routing table's population, the exploration job, the profile feed and the discovery spine keep their existing gates: the router's ability to *use* its NetDB is no longer the condition of its ability to *forget*. The plan **extends** no other plan's guard -- Plan 506's, Plan 507's, Plan 538's, Plan 539's, Plan 540's and Plan 541's lanes and guards stay byte-identical, and the plan's own guard pins all twelve digests. The defect was **reachability**, not mechanism, and the plan did not 'fix' it by widening the store's quota or making the prune faster. The plan's own negative controls found two defects before it closed: the lane was testing `Bootstrap::prune` rather than the production step, and the guard's first rule could not tell a started cadence from a dead one; both were corrected in the evidence. The router's validated store is still the bootstrap's store, and the step's reachability is proven statically rather than behaviourally; both keep their named owner. The delivery stays confined to the controlled loopback profile, the production SSU2 peer-test driver stays unbuilt, the `B` advertisement and `caps=f` stay with their owners, and the stock-peer half stays blocked on Plan 493's authorized two-host topology. **Advertises nothing** -- `router.version` stays `0.9.62` and the inventory's advertised count stays zero |
| **543** | **closed: the participation ledger must not exhaust the exploratory pool** | [the participation ledger must not exhaust the exploratory pool](implementation/network-participation/543-the-participation-ledger-must-not-exhaust-the-exploratory-pool.md) -- closure `plans/closure/network-participation/543-status.md` | The exploratory build driver's peer pool was **permanently exhaustible**. `ParticipationLedger` records how often each peer has been selected, and the selection skips any peer whose counter has reached the cap. The counter **saturates** at the cap, and the ledger's only other release is eviction of the **oldest** entry when it is **full** -- but the ledger tracks 4 096 peers while the profile store's population is the bounded 256-record prefix the reconcile feeds it. The population is a few hundred and does not churn, so eviction never fired and no counter was ever released: after at most 256 x 8 = 2 048 selections the exploratory build driver's selection -- its only live peer source -- was **permanently empty**, and the router stopped building exploratory tunnels for as long as it ran. A participation fact is now a fact about a **window**: the ledger's entry carries a per-peer instant, `reclaim(now_ms)` releases every entry whose last selection is outside the window together with its eviction-order slot, and `PARTICIPATION_WINDOW_MS` is a `const` derived through a `const fn`, so it cannot be configured and cannot be restated. The release rides the clock the driver **already advances**, so no task, timer, listener, socket or configuration key is added. The selection's skip rule and the public `counts()` view are **unchanged**: a released entry leaves the map, so a released peer reads zero exactly as a peer that has never been selected. The counter's cap and the ledger's entry bound are unchanged too -- a peer used recently is still skipped, and the plan changes only how long an over-used peer stays over-used. The plan **extends** no other plan's guard: Plan 505's, Plan 506's, Plan 507's, Plan 538's, Plan 539's, Plan 540's, Plan 541's and Plan 542's lanes and guards stay byte-identical, and the plan's own guard pins all sixteen digests. The defect was **permanent exhaustion**, not slowness: raising the cap only moves the exhaustion later, and shrinking the ledger would evict peers the policy never intended to release. Two candidates from the same survey were rejected as not defects -- the version gate's ordering against the distance truncation is the DHT's placement rule, and the destination build-hop prefix narrows rather than starves. The peer-profile store's feed is still the bounded prefix, the exploratory posture is still not dialable against a public deployment, and the router's validated store is still the bootstrap's store; all three keep their named owner. The delivery stays confined to the controlled loopback profile, the production SSU2 peer-test driver stays unbuilt, the `B` advertisement and `caps=f` stay with their owners, and the stock-peer half stays blocked on Plan 493's authorized two-host topology. **Advertises nothing** -- `router.version` stays `0.9.62` and the inventory's advertised count stays zero |
