- **Managed native app runtime (parallel)**: `plans/subsystems/managed-native-app-runtime-roadmap.md`; Plans 345, 349, 352, 353, 354, and 355 are closed. Plan 368 passed on its contract and daemon bridge (`plans/closure/managed-native-app-runtime/368-status.md`): the runtime-neutral private AppManager protocol, the bridge over the existing principal/capability gateway, ADR 0035, the normative protocol reference, and `scripts/check-managed-app-manager-boundary.py`. **Plan 369 has passed** (`plans/closure/managed-native-app-runtime/369-status.md`): the supervised `i2pr-appd`/`i2pr-apphost` process foundation over the inherited anonymous transport, the app v1 runtime consumer, and 17 black-box tests driving daemon→appd→apphost→fixture→real SAM/I2CP. Its WP5 run found the manager-link flush defect described above, and its negative testing found three guards weaker than they read — including `check-dependency-direction.sh`, which iterated its map instead of the workspace and so reported clean when a member's entry was deleted. **WP4 found and corrected a WP2 defect that no earlier test could observe**: WP2's manager loop decoded inbound frames with the *outbound* control vocabulary while its test peer also sent outbound-vocabulary frames, so the two mistakes cancelled and the test asserted the inverted contract. The two vocabularies share no tags, so against the real bridge the first daemon reply would have ended the transport. WP4 decodes inbound frames as daemon-to-manager, drives its peer in the daemon's direction, and fails closed on any correlated reply for a request the manager never sent. It also added sealed `LaunchAuthority` (no decoder, asserted by method resolution rather than by scanning for a derive), the empty production launch catalog, a concurrent bounded manager client, the bounded instance registry, and the session that maps application stream ids onto daemon handles. **15 mutations were negative-tested; the authority seal makes the crate fail to *compile* rather than merely fail a test.** **Corrective Plan 371 has passed** (`plans/closure/managed-native-app-runtime/371-status.md`): WP2 proved `Supervisor::run` failed **router startup** for any service that never signalled readiness regardless of classification, which made Plan 369 invariant 1 and §5 unimplementable. The generic `i2pr-runtime` fix is a `StartupRequirement::{Required, Optional}` startup-disposition policy orthogonal to `ServiceClassification`, `RestartExhaustion::Degrade` honoured during startup as well as after, a graph-build refusal for the contradictory optional+essential combination, and a `Required` default so no existing service changed behaviour. Plan 369's wrong-magic assertion was **replaced** with the degradation assertion, so a broken manager now degrades only the app runtime. Seven mutations were negative-tested, two of them against real production composition. **Corrective Plan 370 has passed** (`plans/closure/managed-native-app-runtime/370-status.md`): managed-app v1 `hello` had encoded and then failed to decode for every `AppInstanceId`, because a `u128` field inside a serde internally tagged enum cannot be reconstructed from serde's `Content` buffer; it was the only undecodable v1 message. `AppInstanceId` is now a bounded canonical decimal-digit string — Plan 368's already-ratified representation, propagated rather than reinvented — and both protocols now share one id grammar. Round-trip coverage is compile-exhaustive and was negative-tested, and a golden diff across 16 encoded messages proves only the `instance_id` spelling changed. **Plan 369's WP4 gate is lifted; every work package of Plan 369 may proceed.** Package signatures/persistence, secured OS sandboxing, brokered clearnet, UI hosting, and scoped Proposal-170 remain future owners.

record for everything it executed. Plan 268 landed the install-path diagnostic surface
(remember-store, closed 3-value path vocabulary, pre-gate fold, static quarantine, no new
terminal), then executed 8 retained `receipt` attempts on one qualification SHA with zero
i2pr semantic failures: 3 qualified successes against a required 2 (attempts 4, 5, 8),
353 install paths all dispatched with zero bypass and zero catch-all builds, composition
passed with integrity-checked retained evidence. Per Plan 267 §7 the prior distribution
scoped this plan; per Plan 268 §8 all ten criteria pass with executed evidence, including
exact-head ordinary CI (run `36811447204` on `778818b`, all four required jobs green). No
successor plan is registered: none is derivable or required. ADR 0026's one-family M11
experimental qualification is passed and M12 floodfill Plans 270–279 are registered in dependency order behind Plan 269; Plan 277 is stopped at runtime publication/delivery integration, with Plan 278 stopped at its lane boundary and Plan 279 stopped at the Java caps-gated initiation boundary (Plan 306 ready). Parallel Proposal 170 work is intentionally beside, not beneath, M12. Plans 286–297 are `qualified-profile-closed`; the historical Proposal 170/296 and Proposal 170/297 identifiers are subsystem-qualified in [`plans/global-number-collision-ledger.md`](global-number-collision-ledger.md). Plans 319–321 and 323–324 are passed; **Plan 322 passed on 2026-10-05: its canonical RouterInfo gap census is zero** — Plan 339 closed the five per-family selectors and Plan 340 the three transit selectors, with the transit participation posture left disabled and unadvertised, so the honest product baseline for those three is `0` / `0` / `0.0`. **Plan 327 remains blocked**, but no longer for its historical “missing production owners” reason: Plans 341/343 supplied the secret owner and the provider, and Plan 342 wired the request paths; the residual is live failover and post-restart routing (Plan 376). Plan 325 stays blocked as a provider-survey record — the provider question it asked was answered by Plans 330/331, so it is history rather than a live gate; Plan 326 is blocked only on external ELS2 evidence now owned by Plans 374/375 and converged by Plan 377; Plan 328 is blocked on 326/327 (its 322 dependency is discharged). **Plan 339 passed: it reopened Plan 322 for the five per-family `net.status.v6` / `net.error` / `net.error.v6` / `net.testing` / `net.testing.v6` selectors and closed them**, after pinning the i2pd enumeration the Proposal adopts and deriving every code from state i2pr already maintains. Plan 322 was therefore narrowed to the three **transit** selectors, which are a production transit-participation posture change rather than a missing snapshot; **Plan 340 closed them too**, so the census is zero and Plan 322 is `passed`. Plan 327 and its evidence-fixture decision are untouched. Plan 329 passed the Red25519 provenance/normative freeze, Plan 330 passed the independent Red25519 implementation, Plan 331 passed qualification (official vectors plus a byte-identical Emissary differential), Plan 332 passed the type-5 Encrypted LeaseSet2 foundation (no-client-authorization form, daily blinding, lookup secret, B33, opaque NetDB store/serve, client publish/resolve), Plan 333 passed PSK and DH client authorization (byte-identical to Emissary in both directions), and Plan 336 recorded the spec-first conformance decision. **Proposal 170/334 has passed**, reclosed after Plans 337 and 338 removed both of the blockers it originally named: there is now one shared `ServiceTunnelManager` (ADR 0031) and one owner for the service identity store with a rollback that reaches the manager, so a control-created server reaches the publication path and publishes a real type-5 record. **Plan 335 stays blocked** as a historical measured-mismatch record and its Java/i2pd lanes are now owned by Plans 374/375. No Encrypted LeaseSet2 capability is advertised and no live cross-router ELS2 interoperability is claimed: Plans 350/351 closed the local store/serve and consumer-plumbing gaps Plan 347 recorded, so what remains absent is evidence above the crypto boundary, which Plans 374/375 execute and Plan 377 converges. Full Proposal 170 conformance is not claimed; Plan 378 is the only forward plan allowed to make that claim. **Reconciled by Plan 373.**

Parallel anonymity work is beside, not beneath, M12. Plans 297–305 remain stopped historical authority. ADR 0030 removes the unnecessary three-family hostile-reference and mandatory target-isolation prerequisites. Plans 307, 309, 311–312, 314–316, and 318 passed. Plan 308 remains independently blocked on a controlled ordinary-HTTP topology. Plan 310 remains the immutable blocked candidate/path/pool record; its production gaps were corrected by Plans 314–315 without rewriting its closure. Plan 317 remains the blocked record for the qualification-only owner approach; Plan 318 supplies the normal-daemon provider. Plan 316 closed the lifecycle integration, and Plan 311 closed local lifecycle acceptance. Plan 313 passed by converging the pinned-i2pd handshake baseline's single differing dimension. No external three-router i2pd topology is a Plan 314/315/318 closure prerequisite. No M12/mainline readiness changed.

The central invariant — no direct clearnet capability in any path, including failures — is enforced three ways: `OutproxyEndpoint::parse` refuses any outproxy entry that is not an I2P destination, a clearnet target with no provider is a typed refusal, and rules 9-11 of `check-service-tunnel-boundaries.sh` scan both files for socket/resolver/plugin spellings with a positive control. All three static inversions were shown to fail closed. The outproxy clearnet target is a **separate grammar** that shares no parse result with `http::target`, so the direct path's `.i2p`-only enforcement is not weakened. Four defects were caught by this plan's own rows: an unreachable `.i2p` bypass, a bare Base32 label used as a routable spelling, `HTTP/1.0` accepted to a `CONNECT`, and SOCKS5 IPv4/IPv6 reply framing two bytes short. **Not reachable from any request path — infrastructure, not capability.** |
| Proposal 170 / I2PControl | 342 outproxy option surface + request paths + wire lane | **passed (scoped)** | `plans/closure/i2pcontrol-proposal-170/342-status.md` (`passed-loopback-wire-lane-landed-live-failover-rotation-unproven`) | Carries Plan 327's remaining scope, and **found that Plan 327's remaining scope had shipped broken**. All four request-target grammars in the tree (Plan 176 HTTP absolute + authority form, Plan 290 SOCKS5 + SOCKS4a) hard-required a `.i2p` suffix and refused a clearnet authority **before** `classify_client_target` ran, so `CONNECT example.com:443` was answered 403 by the parser and a SOCKS5 clearnet target was answered `HostUnreachable` from inside the negotiator. The step-3 integration was structurally correct and completely unreachable while its unit rows — which call the classifier directly — passed. That is this plan's own inert-acceptance failure one level deeper than anticipated: not an option surface with no route, but a route with no reachable input. Fixed by `TargetPolicy` (`I2pOnly` default, every pre-342 entry point a strict wrapper) plus `ServiceTunnelManager::target_policy`, which derives the parse policy from the **provider registry** so the parser and the classifier cannot disagree. Only the suffix requirement became policy-dependent; IP literals, `localhost`, userinfo and all grammar bounds stay refused. Scoping settled: `handle_proxy_request` **is** in step 3's scope, because the seven-field block is admitted on `httpclient` and a forward path that ignored it would be inert acceptance at sub-path granularity — and because after `build_attempt` a session is a byte pipe to the origin, so it carries the **clearnet** authority in `Host:`, never the `b32.i2p` substitution the direct path makes. **Two more real defects found by running the lane:** the outproxy opener never kicked the delivery driver (`notify_outbound_signal`), so every route would have failed with `TargetUnreachable` in production; and a control commit drops manager-only specs, so an outproxy endpoint must be startup-owned. **Evidence:** the self-composed in-tree loopback lane `crates/i2pr-daemon/tests/outproxy_loopback_wire.rs`, 8/8 rows — the outproxy is reached through **Streaming** via a service server tunnel whose `ServerTarget` is `LoopbackTcp`, exactly as production reaches one, and the fixture never resolves a name so the test process holds no clearnet capability. Guards: `check-outproxy-request-path.sh` extended 24→**39/39** mutations, and new `check-outproxy-wire-lane-evidence.sh` **7/7** mutations with **2/2** deliberate non-flagging controls. **Plan 327 remains blocked**: the live failover rotation between two configured outproxies and a live restart carrying a request are **unproven** — two rows were written and **removed** rather than left ungreen, their names machine-checked in `DOCUMENTED_ABSENCES`. No interoperability evidence; Java/i2pd pins untouched. |

Both resolvers now have non-test production callers. **Two plan premises were wrong and are recorded at the plan that corrects them, not absorbed:** (a) the unblinded hash is *not* derivable from the address — it carries the unblinded **signing** key and lacks the ECIES key, certificate, and padding a `Destination` hash needs, so it comes from the inner record under a signature binding; (b) the address↔record key relationship holds for the **type-7** the publisher emits and does **not** generalize to type 11. | Proposal 170 / I2PControl | 341 restart-safe non-echoing outbound proxy secret owner | **passed** | `plans/closure/i2pcontrol-proposal-170/341-status.md` (`passed-outbound-secret-owner-with-no-routing-and-no-outproxy-claim`; registered `328d201`) | `plans/implementation/i2pcontrol-proposal-170/341-restart-safe-outbound-proxy-secret-owner.md` (`registered-outbound-secret-owner-precedes-any-outproxy-provider`) | The prerequisite Plan 327's own closure record named as its blocker. `ProxyCredentials` is a one-way inbound verifier and cannot produce the password an I2P-routed outproxy needs sent upstream, so no provider is buildable until a restart-safe owner exists. The owner is a runtime-neutral capability trait in `i2pr-service-tunnels` (which may not hold an AEAD) with a router-bound ChaCha20-Poly1305 implementation in the daemon, keyed by HKDF over the persisted router signing seed: restart-safe, non-transferable to a copied config, and no second key file. Plaintext exists only in a zeroizing buffer at header construction. No pinned reference is authority here: pinned i2pd's outproxy is **clearnet-only** (`127.0.0.1:9050` default) with no stored password, and the Java at-rest scheme was not verified this session. Provider, canonical fields, and HTTP/SOCKS integration are Plan 342. |

Adds the owners without changing the participation posture. `TransitParticipation::Disabled` is the enforced production state and holds no counters at all, so it is structurally incapable of reporting non-zero volume; the controlled lane's `Enabled` arm reads counters the real `TunnelData` forward path advances. Honest production baseline is `0` / `0` / `0.0` — what a router relaying nothing truthfully reports. `shareratio` is the one key Proposal 170 does **not** mark *"(adopted from i2pd)"* and that no pinned reference implements, so i2pr defines it as observed transit bytes over attested cumulative sent bytes, labelled as such, with a missing denominator failing closed. Enabling participation stays out of scope: the 2026-10-05 M11 guard correction shows the retained qualification binds SHA `6ab9dc2d`, not this tree. |

The two exhaustions are typed and counted separately: `LookupFinalState::PeerBudgetExhausted` (the policy ceiling) is distinct from `PeerExhausted` (the candidate list running out), a `label()` accessor gives each a stable name, and the daemon's routing receipt gains a per-label `terminal_counts` breakdown so the two never merge into one bucket. A wrong doc was corrected while the vocabulary was touched — `PeerExhausted` was documented as "the lookup exhausted the peer budget", the opposite of what the code does. The behaviour change is on the **failure** path and is stated plainly: a lookup that succeeds within the budget is unaffected, and a lookup that fails now stops at the budget instead of querying up to 32 peers. The measured cost is low — the engine's existing rows peak at **two** attempts, four times below the default of 8 — so no default raise was needed and no existing row changed outcome; the bound was not weakened to make anything pass. **Advertised nothing** — `specs/support.toml`, `Cargo.toml`, `Cargo.lock`, `caps`, the published RouterInfo and `router.version` are unchanged. ## Verification policy

Routine floor in `AGENTS.md` (fmt, check, test, clippy, doc, boundary scripts). Subsystem
evidence lanes are registered in each roadmap §9 and enforced by
`scripts/check-*-acceptance-evidence.sh` + `scripts/check-*-vectors.sh`. Environment-gated
lanes are `#[ignore]`-gated and fail-closed: missing env must fail, never silently pass.
Reference pins are frozen (i2pd `2.61.0`, Java I2P `2.13.0`, go-i2cp `b529ee1c…`).
